### 49 — Welchman engine: a false negative, the middle-ring arm, and two duds

Engine work, not a new wedge. No key, no plaintext, no crib hypothesis changed. What
changed is (a) one class of prior negative is now known to be incomplete, and (b) the
middle-ring gap — printed as untested in every prior phase — is now runnable at a graded
cost instead of a flat 26×.

Every item below was graded against the P1030684 blind control before being pointed at
P1030680, per Phase 11 doctrine. Baseline on this machine, pre-change:
**BREAK, 399 s, 40.0M/s**, 2 raw stops, `AAAH/VYYN`, all ten plugs
(`logs/control-baseline-2026-08-16.log`).

#### 49.1 — `MAX_UPPER` reported clean negatives it had not earned

The Metal kernel builds one upper-involution table per distinct slow-wheel `(l,m)` state
across a menu span, capped at **four**. On overflow it wrote `outSurvivors[lane] = 0` —
the code for *eliminated*. It should have said *undecided*.

Cap raised to 8, and overflow now writes `WELCHMAN_UNDECIDED` (all 26 seeds live) so the
host — which has no such cap — re-tests the lane in full. A lane can no longer be
discarded by a table-size limit.

How often did it fire? `Scripts/max_upper_audit.py` answers this with pure stepping
arithmetic over all 625 distinct catalog spans × 56 naval (middle, right) rotor pairs ×
676 windows — no GPU, no re-running a single sweep:

| Crib length | Placements | Worst-case share of lanes silently eliminated |
|---|---|---|
| ≤ 25 | 564 | **0.0000%** — unaffected, negatives stand |
| 28 | 10 | 0.2853% |
| 30 | 18 | 1.1412% |
| 40 | 33 | **5.5632%** |

Whole-catalog lane space: 78,354 of 23,660,000 = **0.3312%**. Worst observed state count
is 5, so a cap of 8 has margin.

Auditing every fixture the campaign has run shows the damage is concentrated in the
*curated long-menu* arms, not spread thinly:

| Fixture (arm) | Contaminated spans | Contaminated placements | Worst lane share |
|---|---|---|---|
| `p1030680_menus` (Phases 6–7, 14–15) | 61 / 625 | 300 / 2,513 | 5.56% (len 40) |
| `p1030680_fuzzed_menus` (Phase 9) | 12 / 52 | 41 / 400 | 0.71% (len 29) |
| `p1030680_fuzzed_top40_menus` (Phase 10) | 4 / 15 | 8 / 40 | 0.29% (len 28) |
| **`p1030680_top30_menus` (Phase 8)** | **8 / 14** | **20 / 30** | 5.56% |
| **`p1030680_uebung_body_menus` (Phase 16 arm 2)** | **11 / 11** | **40 / 40** | **5.56%** |
| `p1030680_regenbogen_hannibal_queue` (Phase 23) | 0 | 0 | clean |

**Consequence, stated plainly.** Two published clean negatives rest entirely or mostly on
contaminated menus:

* **Phase 16 arm 2** — "40 mid-message ≥16 anchors × 26 right rings … **0** raw stops —
  every menu dead at the board", concluding the Übung-push hypothesis is "exhausted under
  right-ring coverage". **All 40 placements are 40 letters, so all 40 were contaminated** at
  5.56% of lanes, making that negative unearned as originally recorded. **Re-run on the
  corrected kernel and confirmed:** 40/40 dead at the board, 0 raw stops over 6.387×10¹¹
  settings, 18,999 s @ 33.6M/s. The old cap was not hiding a key here. Übung-push stays
  closed, now on evidence.
* **Phase 8** — the curated top-30 turnover sweep, "clean negative … 0 physical 10-plug
  completions … exact register language is exhausted for that curated set". **20 of its 30
  placements were contaminated**, up to 5.56% of lanes. **Re-run and confirmed:** 73 raw
  stops over 4.79×10¹¹ settings — identical to the archived figures — best IC 0.041 / tail
  −4.819, no break. Zero additional survivors from the previously-untested lanes. (The
  scored-completion count moved 0 → 35 purely because the head gate and quarantine's soft
  IC floor both postdate Phase 8; see attack plan 8b.)

Everything at 16–25 letters — the large majority of the catalog, and all of Phase 17's
Thetis-register work — is untouched and stands. Log:
`logs/max-upper-audit-2026-08-16.log`. Re-run fixture:
`Fixtures/p1030680_maxupper_rerun_menus.json` (`--emit`).

#### 49.2 — Middle-ring arm: 6.5–11.4×, not 26×

The middle ring was pinned by a literal `0` in one host tuple, not by any kernel
assumption. The reason it could not simply be swept is cost, and the reason it is cheaper
than 26× is an equivalence:

> The trail depends only on *window* positions — notch tests are ring-free. So if neither a
> lane's middle wheel nor that of its ring-A partner (window `m − ρ`) reaches its own notch
> across the span, the middle wheel steps only on right-wheel notches — the same times in
> both trails — the left wheel never moves, and `offsetM = m − ρ` *is* the partner's window.
> Same scramblers, same verdict. A middle-notch hit breaks the invariant, and those are
> exactly the lanes a middle-ring sweep must actually test.

So ring A runs in full and rings B–Z run only their notch-hitting lanes. The union is
complete coverage, not a sample.

Graded two ways, because a coverage claim that drops keys is the same bug as 49.1:

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Host predicate, 27-letter menu, IV-III-VIII (1-notch middle) | 1,368,224 / 1,757,600 lanes covered (77.8%), **0 verdict mismatches**, cost **6.5×** |
| Host predicate, same, I-VI-VII (2-notch middle) | 1,027,520 / 1,757,600 (58.5%), **0 mismatches**, cost **11.4×** |
| Through the kernel, rings B/F/N, both triples | 74–90% of lanes skipped, **~5,600–6,900 survivors actually discarded per ring**, and every discarded verdict carried identically by its ring-A partner. Violations: covered-but-kept **0**, skipped-but-not-covered **0**, partner-disagrees **0** |

The kernel phase initially "passed" vacuously — at 27 and 14 letters nothing survived
either way, so nothing was compared. It now runs at 10 letters (thousands of survivors)
and refuses to report a pass if it never discarded a survivor.

Then graded end-to-end on the blind control at **full ring coverage** — middle × right,
908,544 shells, 4.152×10¹¹ settings, the first run in this campaign with **no residual ring
gap at all**:

| | Right ring only (baseline) | Middle × right, covered-lane skip |
|---|---|---|
| Shells / menu | 34,944 | **908,544** (26×) |
| Settings | 1.597×10¹⁰ | **4.152×10¹¹** |
| Wall clock | 352 s | 4,307 s (**12.2×** for 26× the space) |
| Effective rate | 45.4M/s | **96.4M/s** |
| Raw stops | 2 | **4** |
| Verdict | BREAK `AAAH/VYYN`, 10 plugs | **BREAK `AAAH/VYYN`, 10 plugs, IC 0.064, tail −2.848** |

The rate doubles because ~70% of lanes exit after the trail test, and 26× the shells costs
only 12.2× the time. The extra two stops are the point, not a regression: with the middle
ring unpinned the true machine has several ring-equivalent representations in the swept
space, and the sweep finds them. Same plaintext, same ten plugs.

Self-test: `--bombe-middle-ring-selftest` · `logs/middle-ring-selftest-2026-08-16c.log`.
End-to-end: `logs/control-middlering-2026-08-16.log`.
**Cost basis for the real arm: ~72 min per placement at full ring coverage.**

#### 49.3 — Throughput: in-kernel plug sieve + GPU seed mask

The kernel applied **no** plug budget; `maxPlugs` lived only on the host, so every
over-budget stop was shipped back and re-derived before being thrown away. And the driver
read only `survivors[lane] != 0`, discarding the 26-bit seed mask the GPU had already
computed, then re-ran all 26 host closures per surviving lane.

Both fixed: the kernel applies the host's `≤10` rule verbatim (so verdicts cannot change),
and the mask is passed back as `WelchmanBombe.test(menu:start:seedMask:)`.

Control: **BREAK, 352 s, 45.4M/s** — same shell, same ten plugs, same IC 0.064 / tail
−2.848, same 2 raw stops. ~13% faster (`logs/control-postchange-2026-08-16.log`).
GPU↔host cross-check remains **0 lane mismatches** (`logs/rehearsal-postchange-2026-08-16.log`).

#### 49.4 — Two duds, recorded as duds

**Exactly-ten-plug budget (`--bombe-exact-plugs 10`).** Kriegsmarine boards carried exactly
ten leads — all three recovered U-534 daily keys and the control have ten pairs — so plugs
the menu has not yet forced must still fit: `26 − determined ≥ 2 × (10 − pairs)`. Sound
(control still breaks, 348 s) and **useless**: on a ghost-heavy set it took 847,434 raw
stops to 847,414 — **0.002%** — while costing 4% throughput (46.4 → 44.1 M/s). The sieve
only bites when a menu determines more than `6 + 2·pairs` letters, i.e. long strong menus,
which already die at the board. Its selectivity is anti-correlated with where ghosts live.
Left opt-in and off. `logs/ghost-exactplugs-ab-2026-08-16.log`.

**Three quarantine flags that never existed.** `--bombe-no-quarantine`,
`--bombe-quarantine <path>` and `--bombe-quarantine-tail-margin` are documented in this
file but were **never parsed** for `--welchman`; the config fields only ever held their
defaults. So the Phase 22 soft-tail arm whose write-up claims a −4.3 floor actually ran at
the default −4.0. All three are now wired (plus `--bombe-quarantine-ic-floor`). A
default-margin re-run of that fixture yields 48 near-misses against the archived 55, which
is consistent with the archived run having genuinely used −4.3 under an earlier binary.
The archived `logs/quarantine_candidates.json` (55 candidates, 2026-08-10) is intact.

#### What this phase does **not** claim

Not a decrypt. Not a new crib. Not a coverage result against P1030680 — the middle-ring arm
is graded and runnable, and as of this entry it has produced **no** P1030680 verdict. The
catalog remains parked at originalIndex 417 (`--bombe-from 418`).

```bash
# Grade the coverage claim before trusting the arm
./.build/release/helut --bombe-middle-ring-selftest

# Audit which prior negatives the old cap contaminated
python3 Scripts/max_upper_audit.py Fixtures/p1030680_menus.json

# The arm itself (expensive: ~908k shells/placement nominal, 6.5-11.4x effective)
./.build/release/helut --welchman \
  --bombe-fixture Fixtures/p1030680_menus.json \
  --bombe-menus 0 --bombe-min-crib 16 \
  --bombe-ring-sweep --bombe-middle-ring \
  --bombe-quarantine logs/quarantine_middlering.json \
  2>&1 | tee -a logs/campaign-middlering.log
```

### 50 — Crib-free path: the Ostwald/Weierud attack, measured

A second engine, on a different principle. Every arm in Phases 0–23 is crib-driven: the
Welchman board needs a probable word, and Phase 13 says every crib we own is imported
register from other nets. Ostwald and Weierud's ciphertext-only attack needs **no crib** —
for each candidate rotor setting it hill-climbs the plugboard and ranks the setting by the
score the climb reaches. Published reach: messages down to about 100 letters, shortest ever
broken **78**, on *three-rotor Heer* traffic. P1030680 is **72** letters on four-rotor naval
M4 — shorter than the record, on a harder machine.

**The wall is not compute, and that matters for how to spend hardware.** 72 letters of naval
German carries ~223 bits of redundancy; a ten-plug board is ~47 bits of nuisance parameter
*fitted per candidate*; the shell × position space is ~10¹¹. Taking the maximum of an
overfitted score over that many candidates is a multiple-comparisons problem, and adding
candidates makes it **worse** by raising the maximum of the noise. Enigma@Home has vastly
more aggregate compute than one Mac and stops at 78. So the decisive quantity is not a rate:

> **margin = climbed score at the true setting − best climbed score over wrong settings**

Positive, and a sweep can find the key. Negative, and the search ranks noise above signal
no matter the hardware.

#### 50.1 — Harness, and a preflight that caught the corpus

`--ostwald-curve` measures that margin at a ladder of truncation lengths over the published
1 May 1945 U-534 keys — same net, same signals office, same German dialect as the target.
Wrong settings are drawn from the *same shell*, differing only in message key: the hardest
available negative control, since a real sweep also enumerates wrong wheel orders and rings,
which are easier to reject. Exhaustion is applied symmetrically to true and wrong settings,
because giving the truth 141 attempts to maximise while each decoy gets one would manufacture
a margin out of nothing.

The harness refuses to print a curve unless its controls decrypt their own published
plaintext under their own published key. That preflight immediately found something:
**32 of 48 controls round-trip exactly**, and the rest do not.

**Correction, same day (Phase 50.9).** That was first written up here as "transcription garble
in the archive, independent corroboration of Phase 22." **That reading was overstated** and is
withdrawn. Classifying the 17 discrepant controls by error shape shows at least three
separable causes, and only the last is garble:

> **Correction, 2026-09-23 — it is 16, not 17.** A standalone verified Python M4
> (`Scripts/enigma_m4.py`, checked to reproduce every clean corpus key) shows `P1030694`'s key
> is **clean**: our decrypt reproduces its entire 52-letter published plaintext exactly and then
> continues for 8 more letters, because the ciphertext is 60 letters and the transcribed
> plaintext was truncated. That is a short transcript, not a key or garble defect. So the split
> is **32 clean keys / 16 genuine discrepancies**, and `P1030694` should not have been in the
> discrepant set. The three-cause taxonomy below is unchanged; one message moves from the last
> bucket to "clean". No break verdict is affected.

* **Multi-part messages with per-part keys.** `P1030675` (719 letters), `P1030688` (472) and
  others exceed the ~320-letter Kriegsmarine limit, so they were transmitted in parts with a
  fresh Grundstellung each. Decrypting end-to-end under one message key *must* produce a
  correct head and a garbage tail — which is exactly the observed shape (errors from position
  298 of 719, from 143 of 208, and so on). Not an archive defect at all; a harness assumption.
* **Indels / transcript divergence.** `P1030711` (98 letters, errors from position 22) is far
  too short for the multi-part explanation, so it is a genuine shift or a divergent transcript.
  Girard documents exactly this in the sister message: an entire four-letter group `HMHY`
  present on the Schlüsselzettel and blank on the plain-paper copy.
* **Isolated substitutions — the real garble signal, and it is thin.** Only **4** controls show
  scattered single-letter errors (`P1030708` 3, `P1030695` 2, `P1030706` 2, `P1030707` 1):
  **8 events in total.**

Eight events cannot support a confusion model, so the prior for any degarbler has to come from
Girard's direct reading of the originals — he names handwriting confusions **U↔N, Q↔G, H↔F**
"etc." — rather than from this corpus. Recorded because the first version of this paragraph
claimed more than the data does. `Scripts/garble_model.py`, `logs/garble-model-2026-08-17.log`.

#### 50.2 — Validation: the implementation is correct

At 372 letters the staged climb recovers **10 of 10 plugs and 100% of the plaintext**, margin
**+1.64**, z **35.6**. At 472: 8/10 plugs, 72% of letters, margin +0.91. That is proof the
harness works, which is what licenses believing its negatives.

#### 50.3 — The scorer matters more than expected

`enigma-cuda` (the reference implementation, which also carries Ostwald's exhaustion) defaults
to scoring sequence `023` = IC, then bigrams, then trigrams. **HELUT's existing climb was
bigram-only.** The staging exists because the measures fail at different times: with an empty
board the text is letter-substituted so n-gram structure is destroyed while IC survives; once
a few plugs are right the n-grams become far more discriminating.

| Length | bigram (what HELUT had) | trigram only | **staged IC→bi→tri** |
|---|---|---|---|
| 472 | 0/10 plugs | 0/10 | **8/10, 72% letters** |
| 372 | 8/10 | 2/10 | **10/10, 100% letters** |
| 252 | 0/10 | 4/10 | **8/10, 67% letters** |
| 160 | 0/10 | 0/10 | 1/10 |

> **Historical model boundary (August 2026).** Sections 50.4–50.8 below are the original sparse-bigram measurements and remain here as dated evidence. They must not be read as current-model rows. The dense-table/objective correction and its dependent rerun are recorded in 50.10.

#### 50.4 — Historical sparse-model length threshold: ~200 letters

Staged scorer, 16 wrong samples per cell, round-tripping controls only:

| Letters | controls | win rate | median margin | median z | plugs | letters right |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 100 | 23 | 22% | −0.113 | 0.82 | 1/10 | 7% |
| 120 | 16 | 25% | −0.127 | 0.61 | 2/10 | 11% |
| 140 | 12 | 25% | −0.080 | 0.88 | 2/10 | 7% |
| 160 | 9 | 11% | −0.136 | 0.44 | 1/10 | 7% |
| 180 | 6 | 33% | −0.056 | 1.23 | 2/10 | 10% |
| **200** | 4 | 50% | **+0.102** | 2.73 | 4/10 | 20% |
| 220 | 4 | 50% | +0.028 | 2.29 | 1/10 | 6% |
| 252 | 3 | 67% | **+0.457** | 5.93 | 8/10 | 67% |

The margin crosses zero at **~200 letters** and median z rises monotonically. Caveat: control
counts are thin above 180 (n = 3–6), so those medians are noisy; the trend is not.

So the honest gap: **HELUT ~200 → Ostwald published 78 → target 72.** We are 2.8× worse than
the state of the art and need to be slightly better than it.

#### 50.5 — Historical sparse-model partial exhaustion: 68% of its 72-letter deficit

Ostwald's named contribution: rather than climb ten plugs from empty, fix one plug and climb
the rest, repeating over every candidate. A wrong greedy first plug poisons everything after
it; one of the fixed candidates is the true plug, and that run starts in a far better basin.
`enigma-cuda` exposes it as `-e` (six letters = 141 distinct fixed plugs after dedup).

A/B at 72 letters, **identical 10 controls, identical wrong samples**:

| Exhaustion | win rate | median margin | plugs | letters right |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| none (empty board) | 10% | −0.2389 | 0/10 | 8% |
| 6 letters = 141 fixed plugs | 20% | **−0.0754** | 1/10 | 8% |

**68% of the margin deficit removed, win rate doubled.** Still negative, so 72 letters is not
yet reachable — but this is the largest single lever found, and it is the one the literature
says it is.

#### 50.6 — The bombe coupling: at 72 letters, 4 plugs flips the sign

The lever only this repository can pull, measured before building the wiring. `enigma-cuda`
documents `-s <plugs>` as "known plugboard connections, e.g. from running a bombe" — and then
requires them typed in by hand, because the bombe is someone else's program. Here both engines
share a binary, and the quarantine tier already stores soft-band Welchman stops *with the
stecker each menu forced* (15–24 of 26 letters, i.e. 7–12 plugs).

Modelled fairly: the true setting is seeded with *k* genuinely correct plugs (what a true stop
forces) while every decoy is seeded with *k* **random** plugs (what a ghost stop forces).
Seeded plugs are immovable, which is the point — they leave the fit. At **72 letters**, the
target's exact length, identical 10 controls:

| Seeded plugs | win rate | median margin | plugs recovered | letters right |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 40% | −0.1503 | 0/10 | 8% |
| 2 | 20% | −0.0991 | 2/10 | 10% |
| **4** | 70% | **+0.1296** | 6/10 | 44% |
| **6** | 90% | **+0.6656** | 8/10 | 85% |
| **8** | **100%** | **+1.0488** | **10/10** | **100%** |

**The margin crosses zero at four seeded plugs; at eight the attack recovers the complete key
and 100% of the plaintext at 72 letters, on every control.** That is below the published
78-letter record, on the harder four-rotor machine.

Read it precisely, because it is easy to over-read. This is **oracle-seeded**: the correct
plugs come from the known key, so it assumes the stop being seeded from *is* the true key. It
does **not** make the attack crib-free at 72 letters. What it establishes is narrower and
still valuable: **a true bombe stop is finishable at 72 letters.** The climber can take a stop
the strict IC/tail bar rejected — because of garble, or short-message noise — and recover the
rest of the board and the plaintext from it.

#### 50.7 — Escalating every stop the campaign ever quarantined: all ghosts

That makes the climber a far better escalator than the GA in Phase 22, which returned 0
survivors at a ~60% coincidence ceiling and could not say *why*. `--ostwald-escalate` seeds
each quarantined stop's forced plugs, climbs the remainder, and reads the result against a
noise floor of random settings at the same shell. If a stop is true its plugs are correct and
the climb finishes; if it is a ghost its plugs are wrong and nothing rescues it.

| Arm | Candidates | Best climbed | Noise-floor best | Δ |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| UEBUNG soft-band (`quarantine_candidates`) | 55 | −3.6583 | −3.4639 | **−0.1943** |
| Regenbogen scuttle | 22 | −3.7316 | −3.3949 | **−0.3367** |
| Hela / Ostpreussen | 7 | −3.7807 | −3.5512 | **−0.2295** |
| Top-30 re-run (Phase 49 8b) | 2 | −4.2270 | −3.4293 | **−0.7976** |

**86 candidates, zero breaks, and every arm's best stop scores *below* the best of 12 random
settings.** Not merely "no survivor above a bar" — the soft band is worse than noise under a
scorer that provably finishes a true stop at this length. The quarantine escalation path is
closed, and closed with a reason rather than a ceiling.

#### 50.8 — Historical sparse-model naval-dialect result

Ostwald names the measure as the hard part, and the generic model is 28.5M letters of the
*wrong* German: Kriegsmarine traffic is a dialect with `X`/`Y` as punctuation, spelled digits
(`NUL EINS ZWO VIR FUNF SIBEN ACHT`), `UUU` for U-boats, `VVV`/`FFFTTT` procedure markers.
64 GB of unified memory invites higher order — 5-grams are 11.9M cells, 6-grams 309M, both
resident — but you cannot *estimate* a 6-gram model from the 7,537 letters of naval plaintext
that exist. So the play was domain match at low order: count trigrams on the 48 decrypts and
mix them into the generic model as a Dirichlet prior, **leave-one-out**, since the controls
under attack are themselves in that corpus and fitting on them would score the model's memory
of the answer.

Same scorer, same staging, model on vs off:

| Letters | generic margin | naval margin | generic z | naval z |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 72 | −0.2735 | −0.2763 | 0.12 | −0.05 |
| 100 | −0.0996 | −0.1048 | 1.01 | 1.08 |
| 140 | −0.1062 | −0.1168 | 0.52 | 0.91 |
| 180 | −0.0114 | −0.0277 | 1.49 | 1.25 |
| 252 | +0.4957 | **+0.5428** | 8.14 | **10.78** |

**Null at every length that matters.** Margins sit within run-to-run noise of each other and
win rates are identical or marginally worse. The one real effect is at 252 letters, where
separation sharpens (z 8.14 → 10.78) — it helps where the attack was already winning and does
nothing at the crossing point. 7,537 letters is too little evidence to move a 17,576-cell table.

Caveat that keeps this an honest negative rather than a closed one: the mixing mass κ was set
to 1200 and **never tuned**. A κ sweep is the obvious follow-up, and smaller κ (more trust in
the naval counts) is exactly the direction that risks overfitting on 7.5k letters.

#### 50.10 — Dense-model and objective correction (current evidence)

Sections 50.4–50.8 are preserved above as the August sparse-model record. A scorer audit then
found that the trigram stage used the 28.5M-letter fixture while the bigram stage was still
compiled from a 10,359-letter sample. Add-0.5 smoothing made the 235 unobserved sparse cells
finite rather than impossible, but collapsed all of them onto the same uninformative floor.
The dense fixture observes 674/676 cells; only `JX` and `QY` remain on their row floors.
Independent Python, Ruby, and Swift implementations agreed bit-for-bit before the table was
accepted. The model is bound as `helut-german-bigram-add-k-0.5-06f8694e-v2`.

The second defect was objective calibration. Objective v1 combined the full
`HostM4Bombe.attackScore`—bigram, IC-distance, and configured crib terms—with exact
hash-bound trigram evidence, but normalized that full attack score with raw-bigram moments
and used raw-bigram/trigram correlation. Its sealed receipts remain historical. Objective v2,
`helut-enigma-search-n72-correlation-discounted-z-06f8694e-e08a5659-v2`, standardizes the
exact attack score against its own frozen-control moments and discounts its paired trigram
evidence using attack/trigram correlation `0.5006502730850204` (production weight
`0.499350`). The old `likeness` label is retired; `bigram-position` is explicitly unbounded
and is not probability or confidence. The final assessment is a separate fail-closed policy
gate: it requires Core bigram-position, IC, and structure evidence plus the exact trigram
model above −3.600, aborts when that model is unavailable, and is not claimed statistically
independent of ranking.

Same 72-letter known-shell control, four checkpoints:

| Metric | sparse August | dense table only | objective v1 (superseded) | objective v2 (current) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Level-1 IC rank | 223,118 / 456,976 | 223,118 / 456,976 | 223,118 / 456,976 | 223,118 / 456,976 |
| letters correct | 4 / 72 | 21 / 72 | 25 / 72 | **25 / 72** |
| true plugs recovered | 0 / 10 | 4 / 10 | 5 / 10 | **5 / 10** |
| plugs proposed | 7 | 9 | 7 | 7 |
| candidate objective | — | — | 5.9117 | **6.7292** |
| truth objective | — | — | 6.8812 | **7.7110** |
| final assessment | no break | no break | no break | **NO BREAK** |

The invariant Level-1 row proves the IC-only sieve was not changed. The dense model moves
recovery from 4 to 21 letters; objective v1 moves it to 25. Objective v2 fixes the calibration
coordinates but deterministically recovers the same plaintext and seven proposed plugs as v1.
The current recovered text remains non-German, and the separate fail-closed final assessment
rejects it.

Current generic versus leave-one-out naval ranking (legacy staged scorer retained deliberately
for comparison; these rows are not publication verdicts):

| Letters | generic wins / margin | naval wins / margin | interpretation |
|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 72 | **5/30 / −0.2306** | **4/30 / −0.2496** | both negative; naval slightly worse |
| 100 | 4/23 / −0.2070 | 4/23 / −0.2241 | both negative |
| 140 | 0/12 / −0.1475 | 0/12 / −0.1397 | both negative |
| 180 | 1/6 / −0.1287 | 1/6 / −0.1381 | both negative |
| 252 | 3/3 / +0.9273 | 3/3 / **+1.1531** | naval helps an already-winning arm |

The target-length conclusion is narrower than the old broad null: naval specialization does
not rescue 72 letters. Its measurable benefit appears only at 252, where both arms already win.

Current oracle-seeding ladder:

| Oracle plugs | win rate | median margin | plugs recovered | letters right |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 20% | −0.1226 | 1/10 | 7% |
| 2 | 30% | −0.1005 | 3/10 | 11% |
| **4** | 50% | **+0.3308** | 6/10 | 47% |
| **6** | 90% | **+0.6656** | 8/10 | 85% |
| **8** | **100%** | **+1.0488** | **10/10** | **100%** |

The sign still flips at four oracle-correct plugs. All five objective-v2-binary stdout rows
are byte-identical to the preceding objective-v1 bundle because this legacy staged scorer does
not consume `EnigmaSearchObjective`; the six- and eight-plug numbers also happen to match their
August values. This is still an oracle upper bound on finishing a true Bombe stop, not a way to
find one and not a P1030680 break. The August partial-exhaustion A/B that removed 68% of its
sparse-model deficit was not rerun, so no current 68% claim is made.

Receipts: `logs/enigma-dependent-rerun-20260907T030037Z/comparison.md` for the compact tables,
`manifest.json` in that directory for exact argv, source/fixture/binary hashes and byte-identical
rerun assertions, `logs/dense-bigram-independent-audit-20260906T220123Z/manifest.json` for the
three-way dense attestation, and `logs/enigma-objective-calibration-20260907T025406Z/manifest.json`
for the objective calibration.

#### What this phase does **not** claim

Not a decrypt. Not a crib-free break at 72 letters — unseeded, the margin there is still
negative and printed as such. Not a claim to have reproduced Ostwald's 78-letter *crib-free*
result: our unseeded threshold is ~200 letters and that gap is unclosed. The 72-letter
recovery in 50.6 is **oracle-seeded** and therefore an upper bound on the real coupling. And
50.7 is a negative about those specific stops, not a bound on the escalator.

#### Levers built vs. still open

Built: staged scoring, partial single-plug exhaustion, the Bombe-coupling model and its
72-letter ladder, `--ostwald-escalate` over every quarantine file, the measurement harness,
the independently attested dense bigram table and the attack-calibrated objective v2.
Open, in expected order of value:

1. **Real Bombe-to-climber coupling.** The current oracle ladder proves the finish boundary:
   four correct plugs make the median margin positive. The missing step is a real target stop
   that supplies those plugs without oracle knowledge.
2. **All-setting phase-two ranking under objective v2.** Level one still ranks the
   true message key 223,118/456,976, so an IC-only sieve can discard truth before the improved
   climb sees it. Avoiding that sieve is computationally larger but now has a calibrated ranker.
3. **Rerun partial exhaustion and then test multi-start/two-plug variants.** The 68% row above
   belongs to the sparse August model. It must be remeasured before it can rank against current
   levers. Naval specialization is no longer the leading target-length bet: current 72-letter
   naval is slightly worse than generic and helps only the already-winning 252-letter arm.

```bash
.build/release/helut --ostwald-curve                       # full baseline curve
# partial exhaustion at the target length
.build/release/helut --ostwald-curve --ostwald-scorer staged \
  --ostwald-lengths 72 --ostwald-controls 12 --ostwald-wrong 6 --ostwald-exhaust 6
# bombe-coupling ladder: 4 plugs flips the margin, 8 recovers everything
for K in 0 2 4 6 8; do .build/release/helut --ostwald-curve --ostwald-scorer staged \
  --ostwald-lengths 72 --ostwald-controls 12 --ostwald-wrong 6 --ostwald-seed $K; done
# escalate real bombe stops (all ghosts as of Phase 50.7)
.build/release/helut --ostwald-escalate logs/quarantine_candidates.json
```

### 51 — The Mulein board: a diagonal board that counts contradictions

Engine work, and the first mechanism in this campaign that the 1945 hardware could not have
implemented **in principle** rather than merely slowly. No key, no plaintext, no crib
hypothesis changed. As of this entry it has produced **no P1030680 verdict**.

**Why relays cannot do this.** On the physical bombe a contradiction *is* current finding a
second path through the diagonal board: the hypothesis short-circuits and the drum spins on.
Copper cannot answer "how many contradictions?" — the relay either lights or it does not, and
every faithful reimplementation inherits that, `WelchmanBombe.propagate` included, which
returns nil on the first doubled row. That is fatal in the presence of garble: a single
mis-transcribed ciphertext letter contradicts a *true* menu and the board discards the real
key without comment. Every exact-crib clean negative in this campaign is therefore a negative
about the **recorded** ciphertext, not necessarily the transmitted one.

In silicon we can count instead of short-circuit, and ask the question a relay cannot phrase:

> **How few menu edges must be deleted before this setting closes consistently?**

Deleting ≤ *t* edges and accepting the setting is the **deletion-tolerant diagonal board** —
locally, the **Mulein board**. It occupies the same structural slot Welchman's diagonal board
did: not a new bombe, a new board on it. Tolerance 0 is the historical board bit for bit.

#### 51.1 — The first implementation was a total no-op, for two compounding reasons

Tolerance must be implemented by **removing** edges before propagating, not by abandoning
them mid-flight. The first attempt did the latter and measured as a complete no-op — one
survivor at every tolerance level. Both causes are worth keeping:

1. **The bad value is already committed.** A garbled edge processed early writes a wrong σ(x)
   into `live`, which then propagates through other edges *and the diagonal board*. Restoring
   the two rows that just conflicted unwinds none of that.
2. **The conflict surfaces at the wrong edge.** Whichever edge happens to be visited when the
   inconsistency becomes visible gets blamed, and that is typically a *correct* edge rather
   than the garbled one, so dropping it does not help and the real culprit stays in.

Enumerating the dropped set is order-independent and reuses the validated exact closure rather
than a second hand-rolled one. `propagateCore` is now the single implementation of the board's
logic, shared by the exact and tolerant entry points.

#### 51.2 — Sensitivity: the exact board really does throw the key away

Known key, P1030684, 27-letter crib at offset 0, corrupting letters inside the crib span
(avoiding self-encipherment, which would make the placement illegal rather than merely
garbled):

| Garbled letters | tolerance 0 | tolerance = g | plugs forced (correct/total) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | KEPT | KEPT | 25/25 |
| 1 | **LOST** | **KEPT** | **25/25** |
| 2 | **LOST** | **KEPT** | **25/25** |
| 3 | **LOST** | **KEPT** | **25/25** |

That LOST → KEPT transition is what makes the mechanism real rather than decorative, and it is
also the check the no-op version failed. The plug column matters just as much: a tolerant stop
still forces **all 25** deductions correctly, and Phase 50.6 measured that a true stop needs
only **4** correct plugs to flip the crib-free margin positive at 72 letters. The two halves
compose — a garbled true key survives the board *and* is finishable by the climber.

`logs/garble-board-selftest-2026-08-17.log`.

#### 51.3 — The Metal port, graded against the host rather than trusted

`board_closure` / `tolerant_closure` in `BombeMetal.swift`, driven by
`--bombe-garble-tolerance` and graded by `--garble-gpu`. Drop-subsets are unrolled as explicit
nested loops because Metal has no recursion, so `MAX_TOL = 3` is structural; above that the
host `propagateTolerant` is the fallback. Cost is `1 + E + C(E,2) + C(E,3)` closures per seed.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Sensitivity, kernel vs host | Identical verdicts on every row of 51.2 |
| Lane-by-lane cross-check | **0/192 mismatches at every tolerance**, across 45 crib × offset × tolerance cells |
| No-op trap (verdicts) | Cleared — kernel reproduces LOST → KEPT |
| No-op trap (work actually done) | Cleared — one 26⁴ shell goes 0.03 s → 0.5 s → ~7 s at tolerance 0 → 1 → 2 |

A useful piece of arithmetic fell out: one shell is 26⁴ lanes ≈ **10 ms**, so tolerance is
cheap per *shell* and expensive per *sweep*. A rings-AAAA pass is ~12 s per menu at tolerance
0 and ~8 min at tolerance 1 (E = 40, 41×); full middle × right coverage is 2.2 h per menu at
tolerance 0 and ~90 h at tolerance 1. **Tolerance is affordable on a pinned or AAAA pass and
unaffordable on full ring coverage.**

#### 51.4 — Specificity: a wrong reading, corrected the same day

Tolerance *widens* what survives, so the price is survivor inflation. Measured on the clean
ciphertext over all 26⁴ lanes at the true shell. At offset 0 the result looked like a crisp
rule:

| crib / edges @ offset 0 | t=0 | t=1 | t=2 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 16 | 1 | **130,787** | 454,052 |
| 17–19 | 1 | 1 | ~134,000 |
| 20–21 | 1 | 1 | ~85,000 |
| 22–27 | 1 | 1 | **1** |

That was first written up as "tolerance 1 needs ≥ 17 edges, tolerance 2 needs ≥ 22."
**Withdrawn.** Varying the placement destroys it:

| Placement | t=0 | t=1 | t=2 |
|---|---|---|---|
| offset 15, crib **16** | 1 | **1** | 268,702 |
| offset 15, crib 18 | 1 | 1 | **3** |
| offset 40, crib 18 | **35** | **4,301** | 369,212 |
| offset 65, crib 18 | **79** | **16,019** | 281,743 |
| offset 40, crib **22** | 1 | 1 | **20,823** |
| offset 90, crib 18 | 1 | 15 | 2,917 |

Sixteen edges costs nothing at offset 15; eighteen edges detonates at offsets 40 and 65. Crib
length does not predict inflation, and neither does the exact-board baseline alone (offset 0 /
crib 16 has `t0 = 1` and still explodes). The governing quantity is the menu's **loop
structure** — tolerance spends redundancy, and only a loop-rich menu has enough to spend.
Note which placements detonate: the ones where the exact board was *already* weak
(`t0 = 168`, `t0 = 2,308`). **Tolerance amplifies an under-determined menu rather than
rescuing it.**

Operationally this is better than a length rule: **tolerance is a per-menu decision,
pre-qualified by measuring inflation against the escalator's budget** (~10²–10³ stops), not a
global switch. The campaign's `--bombe-min-crib 16` cannot simply be run at tolerance 1 — at
offset 0 that is 130,787 survivors per shell against an escalator that has handled 86.

`logs/garble-gpu-t2-2026-08-17.log`, `…-cribgeom-…`, `…-threshold-…`, `…-placements-…`.

#### 51.5 — Ring hypotheses ride one sweep, not one process each

`--bombe-rings AAAA,AACU` pins the Ringstellung to an explicit list instead of sweeping 676,
so a specific guess costs 1× — 1,344 shells per placement per ring, ~12 s. Several rings
belong in **one** run: the ring loop is inside the sweep, so listing them shares the menu
build and keeps a single command queue saturated. Two concurrent processes on one GPU merely
timeshare it and each takes about twice as long for the same finish time. The banner prints
`rings pinned: these are ring HYPOTHESES, not coverage`, and the middle-ring covered-lane skip
is disabled when rings are pinned, since nothing may be deferred to a ring-A pass the run is
not performing.

Graded two-sided on the blind control before use, per Phase 11 doctrine — a flag that pins the
search space is exactly the kind that can silently exclude the answer:

| Pinned ring | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `AAAH` (the ring the control breaks at) | BREAK | **BREAK FOUND** — UKW B / γ / IV-III-VIII / `AAAH` / pos `VYYN`, all ten plugs `BQ CH DI EJ GL KP NV OU SZ TY`, IC 0.064, tail −2.848 — identical to the archived control |
| `AAAA` (wrong ring) | no break | dead at the board, 2 raw stops, no break |

`logs/control-pinnedrings-2026-08-17.log`.

**On the `AACU` hunch.** Recorded because it was raised, and reasoned down rather than run as a
cherry-pick. AACU is the *Potsdam* net's 1 May Ringstellung; Thetis being a separate net means
a separate Schlüsseltafel, so the prior is about 1/676. The training-net intuition actually
points at *dictatable* rings — `AAAA` — which is the most thoroughly tested setting in the
campaign (Phase 14: 868 menus, 3 physical, 0 breaks). And the absorption equivalence means a
rings-AAAA pass **already covers** AACU on turnover-free spans: the control's historical
`AACU/VYAA` is exactly what this engine reported as `AAAH/VYYN`. What remains new at AACU is
only the middle-notch-hitting lanes — the same 8–15% gap any non-A middle ring opens, and the
full middle-ring sweep subsumes all 26 at 6.5–11.4× rather than 676×.

#### 51.6 — Middle-ring arm, resumed

Placement **1 of 24** completed: `NFUNFUUUFLOTTXNULEINSZWONULBEIROTNULSIBE@18` (40 edges, 21
letters, 20 loops), **dead at the board**, 7,947 s at 52.2M/s over 908,544 shells. Killed
mid-placement-2 to free the GPU for 51.3, then resumed with `--bombe-from 2`. ETA ~51 h for
the remaining 23. Note the arm prints only per placement, so a kill costs up to ~2.2 h.

#### 51.7 — Spliced menus: the indel hypothesis, graded

Girard's actual finding was not a substitution but a **missing four-letter group** (`HMHY`,
present on the Schlüsselzettel copy and blank on the plain-paper one). That is a different
failure mode from anything the Mulein board models, and it needed saying out loud: **the indel
board is the Mulein board's second mechanism**, not a separate machine. One board, two
mechanisms, covering the two halves of edit distance — the layers differ and that difference is
operationally load-bearing, so it stays documented rather than collapsed away.

| Error | What went wrong | Fix | Board |
|---|---|---|---|
| Substitution (`U`→`N`) | Wrong *letter*, known position | Delete a menu edge | Mulein board, tolerance ≥ 1 |
| Indel (group dropped) | *Correct* letters, *shifted* positions | Re-index edges, shift step numbers | **Exact board**, new menu geometry |

**The model.** With `R` the recorded ciphertext and `T` the transmitted one, `δ` letters missing
at `T`-position `p` gives `R[j] = T[j]` for `j < p` and `R[j] = T[j+δ]` after. For a crib at
`T`-offset `q`, edge `i` takes rotor step `q+i` (a `T` coordinate — the rotor does not care what
the transcriber wrote) but ciphertext letter `R[q+i]` before the gap and `R[q+i-δ]` after, with
the `δ` letters inside the gap **lost** and their edges dropped. Step number and ciphertext
index *diverge*, which is exactly why this cannot be expressed as an ordinary menu, where
`step == cipherIndex` by construction.

**It needed no kernel change.** `BombeMenu.steps` was already a separate array from
`BombeMenu.ends`, and the Metal kernel already selects a scrambler per edge from `edgeStep[]`.
So a spliced menu runs on Welchman's **exact** board — nothing relaxed, and therefore **no
survivor inflation and no pre-qualification gate**, unlike tolerance.

**Sensitivity (graded, `logs/indel-selftest-2026-08-17.log`).** Deleted a real four-letter group
(`HBSX`) from the P1030684 ciphertext at position 12 to manufacture the transcript a copyist
would have left, then attacked the damaged text with a 27-letter crib straddling the gap:

| Menu | Verdict | Plugs forced |
|---|---|---|
| Ordinary menu, recorded offset (what every prior arm runs) | **ILLEGAL** — self-encipherment | — |
| Ordinary menu on the undamaged text (upper bound) | KEPT | 25/25 |
| **Spliced menu, true splice + delta** | **KEPT** | **25/25** |

The naive menu does not merely lose the key — the misalignment makes the placement illegal, so
the true shell was never even testable. The spliced menu recovers it with all 25 plug deductions
correct, on 23 edges (four fell in the gap).

**Specificity is UNMEASURED on the board, and that is the finding.** At the 16-letter doctrine
floor, **all 28 wrong splices were eliminated by self-encipherment before the bombe was asked**
(0 reached the board). Two honest readings follow:

* *Favourable, structurally.* Enigma never encodes a letter to itself, so a menu built from the
  true crib at the true offset with the true splice is **always** legal — it reconstructs real
  plain/cipher pairs. **Legality can never reject the truth**, while it killed 28 of 28 wrong
  candidates for free with no GPU at all. The splice search is largely self-pruning.
* *But the board is not doing the work.* The discrimination comes from the cipher's own
  constraint, and legality prunes by coincidence rather than correctness. A spliced hit therefore
  needs independent confirmation, exactly as a short-crib stop does.

**Two self-inflicted test bugs, recorded because both produced confident wrong answers.** The
first probe kept the sensitivity crib's start while shortening its length, which walked the crib
off the gap entirely — and since the damaged and undamaged transcripts are *identical before the
gap*, every splice produced the same correct menu and the tool reported "28/28 wrong splices
surviving." The second auto-shortened to 12 letters, below the campaign's own ghost threshold
(menu 627 was 14 letters and produced 193M raw stops), where nothing discriminates. The probe now
forces the crib to straddle the gap and refuses to go below 16.

**Enumeration cost.** One 27-letter crib generates **302** distinct spliced menus (269 straddling,
33 post-gap) against ~25 ordinary placements — roughly 12×. The post-gap family is the strongest
(full edge count, loops 9) and is the cheap first bite: it is the ordinary catalog with every step
number `δ` higher, i.e. "a group went missing somewhere before this crib." Crib-entirely-before-
the-gap cases are byte-identical to ordinary menus and are skipped rather than re-swept.

#### 51.8 — Both mechanisms in one sweep, graded together

Tolerance was a *global* config flag, which contradicts 51.4's own finding that inflation is a
property of the individual menu. It is now **per-menu**: `toleranceFor(menu:)` gates on loop
count and the sieve is set inside the per-menu loop. That is safe with the shell pipeline
because `enqueue` copies the tolerance into the command buffer at encode time, so shells still
in flight from the previous menu keep their own value.

That makes a mixed run possible, which is the right way to run both boards at once.
`--bombe-indel <delta>` folds the spliced family into the same menu list, so one saturated
command queue carries **spliced menus at tolerance 0** — they need none, a splice relaxes
nothing — alongside **loop-rich menus at tolerance 1**. Two separate processes would merely
timeshare the one GPU queue and each take about twice as long for the same finish time.

Graded on the blind control with **both** mechanisms live: 910 spliced menus added (delta 4,
group-aligned splices) plus tolerance 1, rings pinned `AAAH`
(`logs/control-indel-tolerance-2026-08-17.log`):

| Menu | Verdict |
|---|---|
| `…MITU@45` — a **wrong** spliced hypothesis, loops 9, tolerance 1 applied | **dead at the board** |
| `…MITU@0` — the true ordinary menu | ***BREAK FOUND*** — UKW B / γ / IV-III-VIII / `AAAH` / `VYYN`, all ten plugs `BQ CH DI EJ GL KP NV OU SZ TY`, IC 0.064, tail −2.848 |

**1 raw stop over 1.228×10⁹ settings, and it was the truth.** So adding 910 spliced menus and
tolerance 1 inflated the control's stop count by nothing, did not fabricate a false break, and
did not lose the real one. Throughput fell to ~1.0M/s (from 45.4M/s) under tolerance 1 plus
contention with the running target arm, consistent with the 15.3× measured factor.

#### 51.9 — Pricing an indel arm against the target, and the post-gap restriction

Measured rather than estimated. `--bombe-indel 4` on the 24-placement strongest fixture (14
distinct crib texts) generates **1,684** spliced menus, taking the fixture to 1,708 placements.
Spliced menus run on the exact board at full rate, so:

| Arm | Cost | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| All 1,684 × right rings | ~5.9 min/menu → **~7 days** | too much for a first bite |
| All 1,684 × rings AAAA | ~6.4 h | **near-vacuous** — AAAA covers 0/26 at 40 letters |
| **Post-gap only × right rings** (`--bombe-indel-post-gap`) | 237 menus → **~23 h** | the arm to run |

`--bombe-indel-post-gap` keeps only the family whose crib lies entirely *after* the gap,
dropping 1,447 straddling menus. The restriction is principled, not arbitrary:

* It is the cleanest form of the hypothesis — *a group went missing somewhere before this crib,
  so every step number is δ too low* — and it is one menu per legal offset, independent of where
  before the crib the gap actually sat.
* It carries the **full edge count**. Nothing fell in the gap, so no deduction power is lost,
  whereas a straddling menu forfeits up to δ edges precisely when it can least afford to.
* It is ~11–14% of the family, and the straddling remainder is both weaker and roughly nine
  times more numerous — the worst ratio of cost to deduction power in the whole construction.

#### 51.10 — The indel family is **not** a ghost factory (graded), and the first target results

The affordability of an indel arm turns entirely on whether 237 extra menus manufacture false
positives. Tolerance had to be pre-qualified per menu for exactly that reason. Splicing does not,
and now that is measured rather than argued.

**The test, and a wrong first version of it.** `--bombe-indel-only` drops the ordinary family so
only spliced menus sweep. On the **undamaged** control every splice is false by construction, so
any break is a false positive — a clean, decisive test. The first attempt used `--bombe-from 3`
to skip past the true menu and promptly produced a BREAK with correct plaintext, which looked
like a spliced false positive. It was not: the control fixture holds **three** true cribs at true
offsets, so skipping the first merely reached the second
(`VVUUUVIRSOBENNULEINSXXMITUU@1`, IC 0.064, tail −2.867). A legitimate break, and a test
measuring the wrong thing.

**Result** (`logs/control-indel-only-2026-08-17.log`), 97 post-gap spliced menus, all false:

| Quantity | Value |
|---|---|
| Menus dead at the board | **94 of 97** |
| Menus producing any raw stop | 3 |
| Total raw stops | **3 over 5.958×10¹⁰ settings** |
| Survived the ≤10-plug sieve | **0** |
| Soft-band quarantine hits | **0** |
| Breaks | **0** |

Three raw stops in six times ten to the tenth settings, none physical, none even scorable. That
is the specificity claim of 51.7 confirmed on the board rather than inferred: **a spliced menu is
a more specific hypothesis than an ordinary one, not a looser one.** Contrast tolerance, where a
single offset-0 menu admitted 130,787 survivors per shell. The indel family needs no
pre-qualification gate because it does not widen anything.

**First target results from the tolerant arm** (`logs/campaign-tolerance1-strongest-2026-08-17.log`),
right-ring coverage at tolerance 1:

| Placement | Verdict |
|---|---|
| `NFUNFUUUFLOTTXNULEINSZWONULBEIROTNULSIBE@18` (40 edges, 20 loops) | **dead at the board**, 6,675 s @ 2.4M/s |
| `…@21` (40 edges, 20 loops) | **dead at the board**, 11,568 s @ 2.8M/s |

Two of 24 done in ~4 h wall. Rate 2.4–2.8M/s against a 45.4M/s tolerance-0 baseline, i.e. the
measured ~16–19× tolerance cost, consistent with the prequal's 15.3×. **These are the first
P1030680 verdicts the tolerant board has produced**, and both are clean negatives: dropping any
one menu edge does not resurrect a key at those two placements under right-ring coverage.
22 placements remain, ~44 h.

#### 51.11 — Conflict-directed drops: 15.3x → 6.2x, proven sound

The tolerance cost was the blind enumeration. The kernel tried **all** *E* single-edge drops
after the exact closure failed, and most of those edges had never done anything: with `live`
seeded from one bit, an edge whose endpoints hold no live bits is a no-op that writes nothing.

> If the closure contradicts without edge *e* ever having modified `live`, then removing *e*
> leaves the propagation byte-identical up to that point, so the **same contradiction fires**.
> Dropping *e* cannot help. Therefore any drop set that rescues the setting must intersect the
> **active set** — the edges that actually wrote — and enumerating only those is *complete*, not
> heuristic.

`mulein_closure` now exports its active set and `mulein_tolerant_closure` branches on it,
recursively at each depth. On a wrong setting the active set is a short propagation chain rather
than all 40 edges, which is where the win comes from.

**Proven, not assumed.** The host board in `MuleinBoard.propagate` deliberately stays a blind
enumeration, so the existing cross-check is a proof that the prune preserves verdicts rather than
two implementations sharing an assumption:

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Kernel vs unoptimised host | **0/192 mismatches at every tolerance** |
| Sensitivity | unchanged — LOST → KEPT at 1 and 2 garbles |
| Survivor counts | identical (1 at every tolerance) |
| Control menu, *t*=1 | 0.43 s → **0.19 s** (2.3×) |
| Control menu, *t*=2 | 6.01 s → **2.00 s** (3.0×) |
| **Target 40-edge menus, *t*=1 cost factor** | **15.3× → 6.2×** |

`logs/garble-gpu-conflictdirected-2026-08-17.log`,
`logs/tolerance-prequal-conflictdirected-2026-08-17.log`.

**Re-priced arms**, and a structural correction: **C subsumes B.**
`mulein_tolerant_closure` runs the exact closure *first* and returns on success, so a spliced
menu at tolerance 1 tests both "a group went missing" *and* "a group went missing **and** a
letter was mis-read". B is the tolerance-0 special case of C.

| Arm | Hypothesis | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | letter mis-read, ordinary geometry | 92 min/placement · 37 h | **37 min · 15 h** |
| B | group missing (exact board) | 5.9 min/menu · 23 h | unchanged — no tolerance |
| **C** | **both, on spliced geometry** | 37 h for 24 menus | **~15 h for 24** |
| A+C | one sweep, 48 menus | 74 h | **~30 h** |

#### 51.12 — Target arms at durable pause points (2026-08-17)

The edit-distance arms ran **sequentially** because one GPU serialises them. Their receipts
belong to the existing cleartext Welchman/Metal path; they must not be attributed to the new
Verilog Future Bank in 51.13.

| Arm | Durable status | Resume / remaining cost |
|---|---|---|
| Mulein board, tolerance 1, right rings, 24 strongest | **SUSPENDED at 3/24**, all three dead at the board | `--bombe-menus 0 --bombe-ring-sweep --bombe-garble-tolerance 1 --bombe-from 4`; conflict-directed grade reprices the remaining 21 at roughly **37 min/placement, ~13 h** |
| Post-gap δ=4, exact board, right rings, 237 spliced menus | **PAUSED at 144/237**. Entries 1–110 and 112–144 are dead at the board; entry 111 produced 8 raw stops and **0 valid ≤10-plug completions** | Resume `--bombe-menus 0 --bombe-from 145`; 93 menus remain |
| Full middle × right rings, 24 strongest | **DONE under VIII-fast (Phase 58)**; 24/24 dead, 0 stops on placements 2–24 over 1.194×10¹² settings | Other 294 WO open. BLEIBTBESETZT context fixture is **DONE (Phase 60)**: 4/4 dead, 0 stops over 2.076×10¹¹ settings |

The post-gap log has no synthetic completion footer: its durable last result is
`[144/237·144]`, and no target campaign process is active at this sync. A 2026-08-20
resume attempt omitted `--bombe-menus 0`; the default one-menu selection cap rejected
`--bombe-from 145` before evaluating any setting and appended only the no-work diagnostic.
The corrected resume is `--bombe-menus 0 --bombe-from 145`; the durable boundary remains
144/237. Calling this arm RUNNING would therefore be false. Through the completed prefix
there is **no physical candidate and no break**; the remaining 93 hypotheses are open, and
all of this arm still pins the middle ring at A.

Indel went first on merit: it runs at full rate on the exact board, its control specificity is
proven (51.10: 3 raw stops in 5.958×10¹⁰ settings, 0 physical), and it models the error Girard
actually documented rather than one inferred. The tolerance arm buys a stronger local negative:
dead at tolerance 1 means dead even allowing any one crib-span edge to be removed. Neither
partial prefix closes its whole hypothesis family.

One banner correction remains part of the record: `--bombe-indel-only` once printed that on an
undamaged ciphertext every splice is false, so any break is a false positive. That is true on
the control and **false on the target**, whose transcript may genuinely be missing a group.
There a hit is a real hypothesis that must still clear crib-exact ∧ IC ∧ tail ∧ ≤10 plugs.

#### 51.13 — Unified Verilog Future Bank: mechanism landed, target not launched

The campaign machine now has a production Verilog path rather than a collection of independent
host loops. `mulein_closure_seed` evaluates one explicit seed with exact-first one-edge repair,
max/exact plug feasibility, a stable one-hot drop mask, pair/determined counts, live-state hash,
and cycle/closure/trial counters. `mulein_future_tensorlut_top` banks those lanes behind
held valid/ready receipts. One outer TensorLUT lane owns one rotor setting and one shared
80×26 trail; bank slots own explicit Future-Lattice `(a,b,step)` geometry and seeds. The host
compiles indel evidence into geometry rather than asking RTL to parse edit scripts.

The production known-key grade is **P1030684 only**. Four jobs — clean exact hit, exact
negative, combined post-gap geometry plus one-edge repair, and transmitted step 79 — emit
identical full held receipts through source RTL, post-Yosys RTL, clear Yosys-JSON simulation,
and cleartext Float TensorLUT at `BANK_LANES=1`, including two backpressured hold cycles.
This is a cleartext Boolean-oracle grade, **not FHE**.

The finite target inventory is
`Fixtures/p1030680_mulein_identity_postgap_delta4.json`: **261 entries = 24 identity + 237
post-gap δ=4**, with stable evidence/edge provenance and fingerprint
`fnv1a64-32fd2543824a62a3`. Manifest generation compiles hypotheses; it evaluates no rotor
setting. The new artifact has therefore evaluated **zero P1030680 settings** and supplies no
decrypt evidence. Width-qualified synthesis is now complete at `BANK_LANES=1/2/4/8/16`:
45,463 / 92,059 / 189,032 / 369,468 / 758,323 LUTs and 693 / 1,386 / 2,772 /
5,544 / 11,088 DFFs. The corresponding sweep completed successfully in 158.659 / 296.261 /
650.294 / 1,501.129 / 3,814.859 seconds. Those are synthesis costs and graph sizes, **not**
complete-receipt runtime throughput. Width selection remains open until identical workloads are
benchmarked on Metal; graph size alone does not choose it.

#### What this phase does **not** claim

Not a decrypt. The legacy target arms have produced bounded evidence — tolerance 1 is 3/24,
post-gap δ=4 is 144/237, and full middle-ring coverage is 1/24 — but all three are incomplete.
The new unified bank has produced no target verdict because it has evaluated zero target
settings. None of these results asserts that P1030680 *is* garbled or missing a group.
Tolerance removes menu edges; an indel changes Future geometry and runs on the exact board.
Keeping those mechanisms distinct is what makes a combined repair receipt interpretable.

```bash
# Grade the board on a known key with deliberately corrupted ciphertext
./.build/release/helut --garble-board-selftest --garble-tolerance 3

# Resume the two legacy target arms from durable boundaries
./.build/release/helut --welchman --bombe-fixture Fixtures/p1030680_maxupper_strongest_menus.json \
  --bombe-menus 0 --bombe-ring-sweep --bombe-garble-tolerance 1 --bombe-from 4
./.build/release/helut --welchman --bombe-fixture Fixtures/p1030680_maxupper_strongest_menus.json \
  --bombe-ring-sweep --bombe-indel 4 --bombe-indel-post-gap --bombe-indel-only \
  --bombe-menus 0 --bombe-from 145 \
  --bombe-quarantine logs/quarantine_indel_target.json

# Reproduce the unified known-key grade and finite target inventory
make test-mulein-future-tensorlut
make emit-mulein-future-manifest
```

#### 51.14 — Fahrenheit 261: benchmark-selected W4 bounded launch; clean local negative

**Fahrenheit 261** is the formal name of the historical 261-entry canonical-menu/Future
campaign. The name denotes the inventory and campaign, not executed breadth: this bounded
launch reached only shell 0 `B/beta/IV-III-VIII/AAAA`, identity Future 0, and settings
`0..<256`; the other 260 Futures did not run.

The runtime choice is now measured rather than inferred from synthesis. Each bank width ran the
same P1030684 control workload in a fresh process: 16 settings × 16 jobs × 5 repetitions, with
full receipt validation and identical normalized digest `fnv1a64-17435188996435da`.

| `BANK_LANES` | LUT6 | DFF | Median complete receipts/s |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 45,463 | 693 | 233.777350172 |
| 2 | 92,059 | 1,386 | 264.718510307 |
| **4** | **189,032** | **2,772** | **276.035471632 — selected** |
| 8 | 369,468 | 5,544 | 273.889660462 |
| 16 | 758,323 | 11,088 | 230.780129610 |

Selection receipt: `logs/mulein-future-tensorlut-selection.json`; per-width logs:
`build/mulein/mulein_future_bank{1,2,4,8,16}_bench.txt`. The selected graph is
`build/mulein/mulein_future_bank4_lut6.json`, SHA-256
`6a502ae4a21984c6b5e443c6bd14c39c97976dec13ce48401294330af5d31872`, with 189,032
LUT6, 2,772 DFF, 205,177 wires, and 28 levels. These rates measure **cleartext Float
TensorLUT/Metal complete receipts**, not encrypted ticks or FHE.

The protocol-v3 target runner fails closed around that graph. One immutable manifest byte
snapshot is decoded, semantically validated, materialized, and SHA-256-bound with the netlist,
bank width, graph, protocol version, and exact shell/Future/setting plan. The ledger accepts only
an exact ordered plan prefix. Every synchronized chunk carries the full canonical per-job receipt
projection, so resume recomputes its digest and derives hits and BREAK-gate state rather than
trusting summary fields; every persisted hit is host/scoring-replayed. A valid newline-less tail
is repaired in order, while malformed interiors, semantic row drift, duplicate/sparse chunks,
records after a gate, and candidate deletion fail closed. A prior valid gate halts before evaluator
submission and can never print a no-break completion. Atomic non-truncating open plus a retained
nonblocking exclusive lock permits one writer from pre-read through final sync. Metal status,
tags, seeds, configuration, complete counts, held-valid behavior, consume/rearm, and timeout are
also checked. Only exact identity/no-drop replay may enter the discriminator; repaired and post-gap
hits remain non-BREAK-eligible until correction replay or Future-aware full-message geometry
exists. The runner never calls `announceBreak` automatically.

| Launch | Exact scope | Durable result |
|---|---|---|
| One-setting preflight | shell 0 `B/beta/IV-III-VIII/AAAA`; identity Future 0; setting 0 | 1/1 chunk, 26/26 receipts, 0 positives; identical resume skipped 1/1 without append · `logs/p1030680-mulein-unified-smoke-one.jsonl` · run `sha256-389d50312418baf71a98ba8086869daf75b37e5131c553cb94e3c5efb3370a38` |

The protocol-v3 bounded ledger rows completed from `2026-08-20T14:17:43Z` through

shell/Future/setting coverage moved, while settings `256..<456976`, every other shell, the
legacy ring/middle-ring remainders, and physical correction replay remain open.

A second read-only audit paired identity and leading-δ4 Futures by source crib and recorded
offset, then required equal seed, exact/repair class, dropped crib index, plug/determined
counts, and live-state hash. **1,724 candidate pairs** coincide at
`post-gap setting lane = identity setting lane - 4`: 82.25% of identity candidates and
82.57% of post-gap candidates. In this bounded stripe the leading δ4 inventory therefore
mostly resampled translated identity closure states. This does not eliminate a real missing
group at another physical boundary, but it makes blind widening or same-shape δ6/δ8 stripes
low-information.

The next information-gain action is candidate replay, not another broad TensorLUT stripe:
(1) complete and geometry-score the 41 exact post-gap hits; (2) deduplicate and explicitly
replay corrected letters for the highest-constraint identity repairs; (3) inspect repaired
post-gap hits only after those two controls. Sparse IC/bigram/trigram semantics now preserve
holes and excluded edges without inventing cross-gap adjacency, but that reference path is
not yet wired into this campaign ledger. P1030680 remains unbroken.

**Counted follow-up (Phase 53).** Reading this ledger directly resolves the split quoted above:
**41** post-gap candidates are exact with no dropped edge, **40** identity candidates are exact
and already decided (killed by completion), and **4,103** are repaired. So item (1) is a
41-candidate job blocked purely on post-gap coordinates, while items (2)–(3) cover 4,103
candidates that have **no declared bar** — a repaired hit is crib-exact-minus-*k* by definition.
Phase 53 therefore scopes the committed work to the 41 and defers the 4,103.

### 52 — Historian-supplied leads revive the dormant hapax arm

Not a break and not a new mechanism. Two facts have to stay separate for provenance:

1. HELUT had already enumerated the **general recurrence-filter gap** on 2026-08-16:
   `Scripts/relay_crib_mine.py`, `logs/relay-crib-mine-2026-08-16.log`, and the dormant
   `Fixtures/p1030680_relay_menus.json` were committed on 2026-08-17. That audit measured the
   full hapax surface, but its 24-placement fixture was never run and the gap never reached this
   ledger, the public journal, or the campaign report.
2. **Selm Merel Wenselaers** (Historian / Curator, Amsterdam / Antwerp) independently spotted
   the same filter from outside on 2026-09-22, supplied three specific historically motivated
   phrases, reproduced the 29-letter phrase's legal placements exactly, and correctly singled
   out its two useful tied-topology menus. Her correspondence was unsolicited, precise, and
   reproducible line by line. More importantly, it supplied the historical judgement the dormant
   inventory lacked and turned it into an actual experiment. This is joint campaign work: Selm's
   historical selection made the hypotheses actionable, and HELUT's graded machinery measured
   them. The result is stronger because both parts are present; neither is treated as secondary.

#### 52.1 — `--emit-min-messages 2` excluded 99% of the corpus

`Scripts/crib_mine.py` emits a phrase only if **two or more** distinct decrypts carry it —
recurrence as a proxy for formulaic register. The pre-existing relay audit measured the 48
decrypts over distinct windows of length 16–40:

| Window class | Count | Share |
|---|---|---|
| carried by ≥2 messages (eligible for the catalog) | 1,531 | **1.0%** |
| carried by exactly 1 message (**hapax — never eligible**) | 152,938 | **99.0%** |

Those 152,938 hapax strings expand to **2,691,455** self-encipherment-legal target placements,
so brute force is not a campaign. `Fixtures/p1030680_menus.json` is the register *repeated
across* the corpus, not the whole corpus register. The August relay script selected 24
loop-ranked placements from that surface, but no campaign log evaluates them. Its selector is
also not ready to spend the next GPU tranche: adjacent 40-letter sliding windows from one
message consume duplicate family slots, and its final `-vertices` tie-break can favour more
components at fixed edge/loop count — opposite the single-component doctrine learned in Phase 4.

The general gap was therefore **known but dormant**, not discovered in September. Wenselaers'
independent report supplied the missing historical prioritisation and moved three concrete
phrases from inventory into an actual bounded run.

#### 52.2 — Three attested hapax leads, verified

All three exact strings are **verbatim** in the corpus, all three are hapaxes, and none occurs
in the 2,513-placement recurrence catalog or any prior *evaluated* campaign fixture. The
40-letter `STUETZPUNKT…` phrase overlaps two different 40-letter windows in the dormant August
relay fixture; that is prior inventory, not prior coverage, because the fixture was never run.

| Lead | Source msg | Net | Len | Legal placements | Edges | Loops | Single-comp |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `STUETZPUNKTLUEBECKVVVCHEFVIERXUUUFLOTTXX` | P1030662 | second `C/β/568/AAEL` | 40 | **4** | 40 | **19–20** | 4/4 |
| `ALLEINMARSCHNACHJNEUSTADTJANGETRETENX` | P1030693 | Potsdam `B/C/438/AACU` | 37 | **9** | 37 | 14–16 | 7/9 |
| `SOFORTNEUSTADTINMARSCHSETZENX` | P1030669 | second `C/β/568/AAEL` | 29 | **15** | 29 | 7–9 | 8/15 |

Topology cross-checked host-side against the Swift loader on all 28 placements (`comp=` and
`loops=` agree exactly). Wenselaers' self-encipherment filter reproduced term for term: 15 of
44 for the 29-letter phrase, starts `3, 7, 8, 12, 14, 17, 18, 19, 22, 24, 29, 34, 40, 41,
42`; both menus she transcribed by hand (starts 3 and 12) match character for character. Her two
flagged placements are also the *correct* two of the four tied at 9 loops — the only two that
are single-component. Starts 7 and 19 split into 2 and 3 components, the documented
half-board/ghost shape (Phase 4).

**Calibration, which is why these earned GPU time.** The Phase 11 blind-control BREAK menu is
**27 edges / 21 letters / 8 loops**. The 29-letter lead's best placements are **29 / 21 / 9,
single component** — marginally stronger connectivity than a menu that demonstrably recovered
a full key blind. The 40-letter lead at 20 loops ties the recurrence catalog's maximum. These
are mechanically decisive menus, although topology says nothing about historical probability.

Two deductions cut against the leads:

* **Not Thetis.** P1030669 and P1030662 are second-net; P1030693 is Potsdam. Phase 13's
  objection applies unchanged — this is still imported register, and every imported-register
  wedge to date is negative where tested. Training subject matter on a non-training net is
  closer in topic, not in key. The stronger historical fact is that **`NEUSTADT` occurs in two
  independent messages on two different nets** (P1030669 second, P1030693 Potsdam), both in a
  march/transfer construction. `MARSCH NACH ⟨place⟩ … ANGETRETEN` occurs 3× (P1030685,
  P1030693, P1030706), and the `J…J` proper-name bracket 11× (`JKIELJ`, `JSASSNITZJ`,
  `JGOERINGJ`, …), so `JNEUSTADTJ` is idiomatic rather than an inferred correction.
* **One placement is partly redundant.** `STUETZPUNKT…@4` shares **22 of 40 edges** at the same
  target positions with `VVVCHEFVIERXUUUFLOTTXXMITUUU@22`, originalIndex 296, already
  right-ring swept in Phase 15. That does **not** eliminate the new menu: the old menu also
  asserts six `MITUUU` edges that are wrong if this lead is right. Non-nested hypotheses can
  share a long core without sharing a verdict.

#### 52.3 — Exact arm COMPLETE: clean negative, 0 raw stops

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Fixture | `Fixtures/p1030680_wenselaers_hapax_menus.json` (3 cribs, **28** placements) |
| Scope | right-ring sweep, 34,944 shells/menu; min crib 16; quarantine armed |
| Log | `logs/campaign-wenselaers-hapax-rings.log` |
| Result | **28/28 dead at the board. 0 raw stops over 4.471×10¹¹ settings** @ 38.0M/s mean; 0 survived the ≤10-plug sieve; **0 soft-band quarantine hits** |
| Coverage | middle ring **pinned A** — residual gap ~8% (1-notch) / ~15% (2-notch) at 29–40 letters |
| Status | **DONE as a clean local negative** for these 28 exact placements under right-ring coverage |

Zero raw stops is the strongest form of this negative: not one setting in 4.47×10¹¹ survived
the contradiction test anywhere, so nothing even reached the plug sieve or the discriminator.
These three exact strings, at these 28 placements, are not in the **recorded** ciphertext under
right-ring coverage.

#### 52.5 — Post-gap δ=4 arm COMPLETE: second clean negative

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Scope | 28 spliced menus (308 straddling dropped), exact board, right-ring sweep, middle ring A |
| Log | `logs/campaign-wenselaers-postgap-delta4-rings.log` |
| Result | **28/28 dead at the board. 0 raw stops over 4.471×10¹¹ settings** @ 37.8M/s; 0 survived the ≤10-plug sieve; **0 quarantine hits** (no file written) |
| Status | **DONE as a clean local negative** |

The settings count is **identical** to the exact arm's 447,119,941,632, which is the expected
consistency check rather than a coincidence: a post-gap splice preserves the crib and shifts every
step number by δ, so the menu count, edge counts and shell count are unchanged. Only the rotor
coordinates move.

**Both transcript readings are now closed for these three phrases at these placements.** Neither
"the recording is faithful" (52.3) nor "a four-letter group went missing before the crib" (52.5)
admits a single surviving setting under right-ring coverage. Zero raw stops in either arm — nothing
even reached the plug sieve.

What remains untested for these phrases is narrow and printed: **tolerance 1** (measured affordable
at 7.7× / ~19.7 h with 0 projected stops, 52.4) and the **middle-ring gap**. A clean exact and
post-gap negative does not close a mis-read *letter*, which is what tolerance tests.

#### 52.4 — Transcript-error arms: measured, both affordable

The clean exact negative sharpens the remaining hypothesis rather than closing it. If any of
these phrases really is present, then the recorded transcript differs from what was
transmitted — which is precisely the Mulein board's two mechanisms (Phase 51). Both were
priced against this fixture rather than estimated:

| Arm | Menus | Mechanism | Measured cost | Inflation risk |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Post-gap δ=4, exact board | **28** (308 straddling dropped) | a 4-letter group went missing *before* the crib, so every step number is δ too low | full rate, **~3 h** | **none** — a splice relaxes nothing; control specificity 3 raw stops / 5.958×10¹⁰ settings, 0 physical (Phase 51.10) |
| Tolerance 1, ordinary geometry | **24/24 qualify** | any one crib-span edge may be a mis-read letter | **7.7×** factor, **~19.7 h** | **projected 0 stops** — measured, not assumed |

The tolerance prequal result is worth recording because it **corrects a plausible prior**.
Phase 51.4 warns that tolerance amplifies under-determined menus, so the 29-letter
`SOFORT…` placements at 5–9 loops (several `comp=2`/`comp=3`) looked like the risky ones. They
are not: every menu projects **0** stops at tolerance 1. The reason is visible in 52.3 — a menu
that contradicts *everywhere* at tolerance 0 has no surviving neighbourhood for tolerance to
widen. Measurement beat the heuristic, which is what the prequal gate exists for.

**One sweep, not two.** `mulein_tolerant_closure` runs the exact closure first and returns on
success, so a spliced menu evaluated at tolerance 1 tests **both** edit mechanisms at once —
group-missing, and group-missing *plus* a mis-read letter. Per-menu tolerance (Phase 51.8) lets
one saturated command queue carry spliced menus at tolerance 0 alongside ordinary menus at
tolerance 1. Two processes on one GPU merely timeshare it.

#### What this phase does **not** claim

Not a decrypt, key, or plaintext. The exact negative covers **28 placements of 3 strings under
right-ring coverage with the middle ring pinned A**; it is not a statement about the phrases in
general, about other placements, or about the 2.69M-placement hapax surface. The August inventory
predates the correspondence, but Selm's independent diagnosis, specific leads, exact placement
arithmetic, and menu-selection insight are what made that dormant surface actionable. The two clean
runs are joint campaign findings: her historical prioritisation defined a worthwhile test and
HELUT's graded path executed it. The leads remain second-net/Potsdam register, so Phase 13's
objection stands and the prior on presence is low.
A clean exact negative explicitly does **not** close the garble hypothesis (Phase 51.2), which
is why 52.4 exists. The post-gap δ=4 arm is now also a clean 28/28 negative (Phase 52.5);
**tolerance 1 is the sole remaining edit arm for these phrases** and would still have to clear
crib-exact ∧ IC ≥ 0.055 ∧ tail > −3.600 ∧ ≤10 plugs.

#### 55.2 — Source-reported primary evidence, held at the right grade

The following reports carry named primary references and materially change priors, but the scans,
page references, or independent transcriptions are not in this workspace. They are therefore
printed as **source-reported**, not promoted into “What we know for sure” without qualification:

* **DEFE3/577 ZTPG 367835 (26 April):** reported 15-boat Volksliste III cohort ordered to
  Travemünde; five men per boat to remain as demolition crews, remaining armed crews to the
  3. U-Lehrdivision at Neustadt.
* **Original KTB U-3521:** reported preparations for scuttling on 30 April and 1 May; on 2 May the
  crew leaves except for the `Sprengkommando` and travels to Neustadt. A 9 April entry reportedly
  reads `Auf Sehrohrtiefe, (FT-Programmzeit)`, grounding scheduled radio reception during
  AGRU-Front Baltic training.
* **DEFE3/579 ZTPG 369048 (1 May):** reported joint arrival of U-3014, U-3024 and U-2538 at
  Travemünde; P1030707 carries the same cluster in corrupted solved traffic.
* **Same-day radio language:** `KLAR ZUM VERSENKEN GEM. BEFEHL.` is reported from another message,
  but no message ID or exact raw normalisation has yet been supplied.
* **Allocation-list chronology:** a preserved list called `Forelle` reportedly assigns columns
  641–670 to Aegir and 671–703 to Thetis, while the later list displayed by Hörenberg assigns
  621–653 to Thetis. A fixed K-Buch column plus changing allocation lists can reconcile the
  apparent column-645 contradiction. The exact date, codename, and provenance of the later list
  remain pending, so **M-Thetis stays the working assignment rather than an unqualified confirmed
  chronology**.

Archive enquiries are active at the Deutsches U-Boot-Archiv, Deutsches Schifffahrtsmuseum,
Big Heritage / the U-534 collection, and around the privately held U-3521 KTB. Requested targets:
Funkkladden, Funkbücher, Funkspruchzettel, Schlüsselzettel, `M-Thetis` / `M Tht` /
`U-Bootübungsschlüssel`, and the exact Funkprogramm behind `Programmzeit`. No reply is evidence
until it arrives.

#### 55.3 — Historical interpretation: what moves and what stays open

1. **Neustadt and scuttling are linked, not competing scenarios.** The reported process leaves a
   demolition party aboard while the rest of the crew transfers to Neustadt. Do not force
   `NEUSTADT` and `VERSENKUNG` into separate plaintext banks, and do not concatenate them into an
   invented sentence either. Let independently attested strings compete independently.
2. **AGRU-Front is not simply Thetis.** Scheduled FT reception during Baltic training makes a
   broadcast layer concrete, but Selm reports AGRU-Front/FdU Ausbildung messages in DEFE3 on more
   than one communications layer. `AGRU-Front = Thetis` is explicitly rejected.
3. **Recovery provenance is not recipient identity.** P1030680 survives among U-534 papers; that
   does not prove it was addressed to U-534. An intercepted signal for another training boat or
   group is now an OPEN prior, which raises short addressing/boat-number material relative to a
   polished free-standing order.
4. **No clean boat-number claim survived rechecking.** The proposed
   `UUUDREINULZWOVIER` / `UUUZWOFUENFDREIACHT` exact attestations are **withdrawn**. P1030704 and
   P1030707 preserve complementary corrupted material, not either clean string. Turing does not
   get to eat reconstructed boat numbers as exact cribs.
