### 54 — Where the probability actually is (measured, not asserted)

A strategy entry. No new coverage; it re-reads two things the campaign has been carrying as
settled and finds one of them overstated.

Decompose the campaign's own odds:

> `P(break) = P(correct crib is in our set) × P(key lies in covered ring space) × P(we ran that placement)`

Years of GPU have gone into the third term. The second has a **known, measured hole**: with the
middle ring pinned at A, keys whose middle wheel turns over inside the crib span are unreachable —
~8% at one notch, ~15% at two (Phase 12). Against P1030680 that arm stands at **1/24 placements**.
The first term is the one Phase 13 says is near zero.

#### 54.1 — Phase 13 is right about provenance and overstated as applied

Phase 13 states every crib is "imported register from other nets," which is true of *provenance*.
It has been applied as though the whole catalog were Potsdam idiolect. Classifying the **868**
catalog placements of length ≥16 by what they actually contain:

| Register class | Placements | Single-component ∧ loops ≥ 8 |
|---|---:|---:|
| **Universal Kriegsmarine procedure only** (spelled numerals, `VVV`, `UUU`, `FLOTTX`, `VONVON`) | **736** | **288** |
| Contains net-specific addressing (`KOMXADM`, `CHEF`, place names, `AUSB`) | 132 | 9 |

So **85% of the tested catalog is not net-specific at all.** `UUUFLOTTX`, `NULEINSZWONUL`, `VVV`
and spelled numerals are Kriegsmarine-wide conventions; they are not evidence of Potsdam. And the
strongest of them are about **U-boat flotilla movements in the Baltic**, which is precisely what a
Baltic training net existed to coordinate. The register objection bites hard on openings and
addressing, and much more weakly on numerals and procedure markers.

That materially raises `P(correct crib is in our set)` for the universal subset — and those 288
strong placements have had **no middle-ring coverage whatsoever**. The existing
`Fixtures/p1030680_maxupper_strongest_menus.json` is already pointed at the most connected of
them, which is why the right action is to **resume** that arm rather than build a new one.

#### 54.2 — The hapax surface, priced honestly

Phase 52 opened the one-carrier surface. Measured with the connectivity lesson from 53.6:

| Filter | Count | Cost × right rings |
|---|---:|---|
| all legal hapax placements | 2,691,455 | ~12,000 days |
| single-component ∧ loops ≥ 8 | **714,142** | ~3,200 days |
| **reduced to (source, alignment-diagonal) hypotheses** | **7,321** | — |
| …of those loops ≥ 8 | **5,497** | **~25 days** |
| …of those loops ≥ 16 | **3,600** | ~16 days |

Connectivity filtering alone does **not** make this tractable — that expectation was wrong, and
714,142 is the refutation. What does is recognising that a window slid by one letter is **not a new
hypothesis**: it asserts the same source text at the same alignment. Grouping by
`(source message, source_start − target_offset)` and keeping the strongest menu per diagonal is a
large reduction and turns weeks-of-decades into weeks. That, not raw enumeration, is how this
surface becomes runnable — and it is the concrete form of the selector repair named in Phase 52.

**Reconciliation, 2026-09-23 — the collapse is now tooling, and two of five rows above do not
reproduce.** The lever was only ever applied by hand here; it is now
`Scripts/menu_diagonal_collapse.py --use-provenance`, with `relay_crib_mine.py` emitting the
`sourceMessage` / `sourceStart` it previously computed and discarded (which is *why* no consumer
could collapse). Re-measured from `Fixtures/u534_corpus.json` at window lengths 16–40:

| Row | This table | Re-measured | Status |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| all legal hapax placements | 2,691,455 | **2,691,455** | **exact** |
| single-component ∧ loops ≥ 8 | 714,142 | **714,142** | **exact** |
| (source, alignment-diagonal) hypotheses | 7,321 | **8,779** | **+20% — unreconciled** |
| …of those loops ≥ 8 | 5,497 | 6,036 | +10% — unreconciled |
| …of those loops ≥ 16 | 3,600 | 3,699 | +3% — unreconciled |

Receipt: `python3 Scripts/hapax_collapse_receipt.py --check` recomputes all five rows and fails if
any drifts.

Both *placement* counts reproduce to the digit, so the enumeration, the self-encipherment filter,
the 16–40 length range and the connectivity gate are all confirmed. Every *diagonal* count is
10–20% high. Eliminated as causes: windows occurring at multiple starts inside their carrier
(**zero**, and note this had to be counted as a set of distinct `(source, start)` pairs — a counter
incremented during ingestion re-sights the same pair once per enclosing length and reports spurious
ambiguity, which is a trap this receipt hit and now documents), the miner's existing-catalog
exclusion (its 98 texts are recurrent, never hapax, so the pool is unchanged), the length range
(placements would move and do not), and keying on diagonal alone rather than `(source, diagonal)`
(that gives 755, not 7,321). The surface has 48 contributing sources and the per-source diagonal
count tracks source length as expected.

So the archived 7,321 came from a grouping implementation that is **not in this repository** —
Phase 54.2 printed the numbers without committing the script that produced them. The honest
position: **8,779 is the reproducible figure** (command above, re-runnable), the 130× headline is
consequently a **≈307×** reduction on the raw surface, and the archived trio stays printed rather
than silently overwritten because we cannot yet say which of the two is wrong. The conclusion the
row was drawn for is unaffected in direction — the collapse is the difference between ~12,000 days
and weeks either way — and the cost column should be re-derived from the reproducible counts before
any hapax GPU tranche is scheduled. This is a bookkeeping discrepancy, not a coverage claim: no
setting was evaluated and no verdict moved.

#### 54.4 — Crib-free: the 68% lever verified, single-plug exhaustion saturated, depth 2 lands

Host-side, no GPU contention. Receipts: `logs/ostwald-exhaust-rerun-dense.log`,
`logs/ostwald-exhaust-ladder-dense.log`, `logs/ostwald-exhaust-depth2-ab.log`.

**The August 68% claim is verified, not withdrawn.** Rerun under the repaired dense scorer at 72
letters, `--ostwald-exhaust 6` gives median margin **−0.0672** against the unseeded dense baseline
of −0.2306 (30 controls) — about **71%** of the deficit removed, slightly better than the archived
sparse-model −0.0754. Phase 50.10's "no current 68% claim is made" can be retired: the lever is
real under the current scorer.

**Deeper single-plug exhaustion saturates — a clean negative worth having.** Sweeping the hot-letter
count on a fixed 12-control cell:

| `--ostwald-exhaust` | win % | median margin | median z |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 6 | 20% | −0.0672 | −0.34 |
| 8 | 38% | −0.0672 | −0.34 |
| 10 | 38% | −0.0567 | **+0.70** |
| 13 | 38% | −0.0567 | +0.70 |
| 26 (all 325 plugs) | 38% | −0.0567 | +0.58 |

Nothing past ~10 letters. The mechanism is the campaign's own multiple-comparisons argument:
exhaustion is applied **symmetrically** to true and wrong settings, so extra starts lift the decoys'
best score too. Do not spend further compute widening single-plug exhaustion. Note the median z
turning **positive** — the truth now outranks the decoy *mean*, just not the decoy *maximum*, which
is exactly the overfitting signature Phase 50 describes.

**Depth 2 implemented and measured.** `--ostwald-exhaust-depth 2` fixes two **disjoint** plugs and
climbs the rest. The reason to expect anything is asymmetry rather than compute: among depth-1
starts the truth gets one whose fixed plug is genuinely correct, and among depth-2 starts one whose
*pair* is correct — and basin quality pays steeply (oracle ladder: 2 correct −0.10, 4 correct
+0.33). Same 12-control cell, `--ostwald-exhaust 6`:

| Arm | win % | median margin |
|---|---:|---:|
| unseeded | 20% | −0.1226 |
| depth 1 | 20% | −0.0672 |
| **depth 2, exhaust 6** | **30%** | **−0.0361** |
| depth 2, exhaust 10 | 40% | −0.0486 |

Monotone, and it removes ~46% of what depth 1 left. **Still negative — this is not a break**, and
the caveat matters: at 10 usable controls a 0.03 shift and a 20%→30% win change is one control, so
the direction is consistent with the mechanism while the magnitude is only weakly supported. It
needs more controls before it carries weight. The later `--ostwald-exhaust 10
--ostwald-exhaust-depth 2` cell **did complete**: 40% wins / median margin **−0.0486**. Its win
count gains one control, but its median margin is *worse* than exhaust-6 depth 2 (−0.0361), so
widening the depth-2 start set also saturates under multiple comparisons. Do not spend more host
compute increasing the hot-letter count; depth 2 at 6 is the better measured point, still negative.

**A method note, recorded because it nearly became a false negative.** The first depth-2 A/B used
shell word-splitting to drive four cells and every cell returned an identical −0.1226 — exactly the
*unseeded* baseline. The flags had never taken effect. It was caught only because four independent
cells agreeing to four decimals is not a thing that happens. Explicit invocations replaced the
loop. A measurement harness that silently ignores a malformed flag will manufacture agreement.

**What none of this is.** No route here is likely to break the message this month; each buys a real
but small daily probability. Depth 2 is the most promising measured lever and it is still 0.036
short of zero on a thin sample. The only step change available is **archival**: one Thetis plaintext
fragment would be worth more than every arm above, because it would replace a guessed crib with a
known one. That is why external correspondence is worth cultivating, and it is the honest answer to
"how do we break this."

### 53 — Decision: adjudicate the 41, defer the 4,103

A planning entry, not a result. It records **why** the next engineering step is the
geometry-aware adjudicator and **what it may not claim**, before any code is written.

#### 53.1 — The candidate population, counted rather than quoted

Phase 51.16 left 4,184 host-verified physical candidates ungraded. Counting the durable
ledger directly (10,048 rows, read-only) resolves the split the prose asserted:

| Class | Count | Gradeable today? |
|---|---:|---|
| `identity`, exact, no drop | **40** | **Already graded** — all `exact-identity-killed-by-completion` |
| `post-gap-delta4`, **exact, no drop** | **41** | **No, and this is the target.** Blocked only by coordinates |
| `identity`, repaired (non-exact, ≥1 dropped edge) | 2,056 | No — needs a *new declared bar* |
| `post-gap-delta4`, repaired | 2,047 | No — needs a *new declared bar* |

So "4,184 ungraded candidates" is misleading as a work item. **81 are exact, 40 of those are
already decided, and the actual adjudicable prize is 41 candidates.** The remaining **4,103**
are repaired, and their blocker is not code.

#### 53.2 — Why the 41 are different from the 4,103

`PostBombeDiscriminator.isBreak` opens with `guard GermanTrigrams.isLoaded, candidate.cribExact`.

* The **41** are exact with no dropped edge, so `cribExact` is a meaningful question for them.
  They fail only because `decrypt` builds one `EnigmaM4Key` and calls `processText` over a dense
  array, while post-gap δ=4 puts every transmitted step after the gap 4 higher than its recorded
  index. The decrypt is wrong by construction across the tail, and `cribExact`/`tailScore` index
  transmitted coordinates into a recorded array. That is a **coordinate bug, and it is fixable.**
* The **4,103** are repaired: a repaired candidate is crib-exact-minus-*k* **by definition** —
  that is what the repair *is*. There is no threshold, flag, or scorer that makes `cribExact` true
  for them. Grading them requires **declaring a new bar** (crib-exact on retained edges plus a
  printed repair count, or soft-band quarantine only). That is a claim-sheet decision requiring
  human sign-off, not an implementation task, and it is explicitly **deferred**.

#### 53.3 — Shape of the work, and the constraint that fixes it

The existing campaign ledger **must not be modified.** `p1030680ValidateChunkRecord` re-derives
every candidate through `p1030680ReplayCandidate` and compares with struct equality, so adding
score fields to the 4,184 existing rows would fail all 4,184 equality checks and the ledger would
refuse to open. Adjudication is therefore a **separate, read-only** pass writing its **own**
ledger — which is the right shape anyway, and preserves the single-writer lock so a resumed
campaign is never blocked.

Everything else needed already exists and is tested: `MuleinSparseReferenceEvaluator`
(validate → `accumulateIC` → `traceAdjacency` → `evaluateNGrams`), the sparse transcript data
model with holes/excluded-edge barriers, the frozen add-0.5 German bigram/trigram adapters, the
host board, and `EnigmaM4Machine.process` as the per-step primitive. The ledger persists enough
per candidate — shell, setting lane, positions, seed, Future ordinal **and** content ID, family,
`exact`, dropped-edge mask and IDs, pair/determined counts, `liveHash` — to replay and score
**without touching the GPU**. The manifest is content-addressed in every row, so a rebuild can be
proven identical.

**The one genuinely missing piece** is a producer: `MuleinSparseTranscript(` is constructed only
inside tests. Nothing builds one from real campaign state.

| Step | Work | Risk |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `MuleinSparseTranscriptBuilder` — walk transmitted steps, burn a rotor step at each hole, emit `.physicalHole`/`.excludedEdge` barriers, crib steps as barriers so the tail stays a tail | mechanical |
| 2 | **P1030684 sparse calibration control** — exact / synthetic δ=4 / one-edge repair; prove the true key separates from its ghosts *through the sparse path* | **this is the gate** |
| 3 | Read-only `P1030680CandidateAdjudicator` + ledger-schema visibility refactor + CLI flag; new output ledger with its own digest chain | medium |
| 4 | Run against the 41 | minutes, no GPU |

#### 53.4 — Steps 1–2 DONE: builder landed, calibration gate PASSED

`MuleinSparseTranscriptBuilder` (the missing producer) and
`MuleinSparseCalibrationControl` are in. `--sparse-calibration-control` ·
`logs/sparse-calibration-control.log`.

**The decisive test and its result.** A real four-letter group (`HBSX`) was deleted from the
P1030684 ciphertext at recorded position 12, taking the recording from 120 to 116 letters while
the transmitted timeline stays 120. Attacking that damaged transcript with post-gap geometry and
the **true** key:

| Arm | Geometry | Key | IC | sparse tri | eligible | runs | barriers | plaintext |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| A | exact | TRUE | 0.0639 | **−2.8470** | 120 | 1 | 0 | **EXACT** |
| B | exact | wrong ×8 | ~0.040 | −4.98…−5.03 | 120 | 1 | 0 | — |
| C | **post-gap δ4** | **TRUE** | 0.0625 | **−2.8689** | 116 | **2** | **4** | **EXACT** |
| D | post-gap δ4 | wrong ×8 | ~0.040 | −5.02…−5.08 | 116 | 2 | 4 | — |
| E | exact, 1 edge excluded | TRUE | 0.0642 | −2.8513 | 119 | 1 | 1 | — |

Arm C is the point: **the true key reproduces the plaintext exactly across the gap.** If
hole-burning were wrong, every symbol after position 12 would be garbage, because the rotor would
be four steps behind for the rest of the message. Exact recovery of 116 letters in two runs
separated by four barriers is a far stronger claim than any score. Separation is
**+1.8445** (worst TRUE −2.8689 vs best WRONG −4.7134).

#### 53.5 — A correction: the dense bar *does* transfer

Phase 53 first recorded that comparing a sparse mean to −3.600 would be a "category error" and
that a new floor had to be published. **That was wrong, and the control disproves it.**

`GermanTrigrams.scoreIfLoaded` returns `total / (n − 2)` — a *mean log-probability per trigram
window*. The sparse path's `meanLogProbability` is the same quantity over the windows geometry
permits. On hole-free geometry the two agree **bit-exactly**:

| Quantity | Dense | Sparse | \|Δ\| |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| trigram | −2.847037 | −2.847037 | **0.00e+00** |
| IC | 0.063866 | 0.063866 | **0.00e+00** |

So the sparse statistic **is** the dense statistic, and `breakThreshold −3.600` / `icFloor 0.055`
are comparable in scale. No new threshold is needed, which removes the one part of the plan that
looked like it might require a fresh claim-sheet row.

Two caveats survive, and neither is a scale problem:

* the dense `tailScore` excises crib positions and **concatenates** the remainder, inventing
  adjacency across the excision; the sparse path refuses that bridge. A sparse tail is therefore a
  different *sample* of the same statistic, not a different unit. v1 reports whole-transcript
  sparse scores and does **not** fabricate a crib-excluded sparse tail — the barrier taxonomy has
  no legal "crib constraint" reason, and inventing one by reusing `.excludedEdge` would misreport
  a crib letter as a deleted edge. Adding `MuleinPlaintextBarrier.cribConstraint` is a deliberate
  model change for later, not a shortcut now;
* fewer eligible windows makes the estimate noisier, so **window count is reported beside every
  score** and a barrier-heavy candidate earns less confidence at the same mean.

#### 53.6 — Step 3 DONE: all 41 adjudicated, and 51.16's diagnosis was wrong

`P1030680SparseAdjudicator` (`--sparse-adjudicate`) reads the durable ledger with **no write lock**
and **evaluates no rotor setting**. Receipts: `logs/p1030680-sparse-adjudication.log`,
`logs/p1030680-sparse-adjudication.jsonl`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Source ledger | `logs/p1030680-mulein-operational-settings-000001-000256-v3.jsonl`, SHA-256 `c516238d…1e7e52f2` |
| Manifest | rebound **by content**: 628 Futures, SHA-256 match, fingerprint `fnv1a64-616326e94036a97d` |
| Rows decoded | 10,048, single run identity `sha256-efb770bc…` |
| Adjudicable | **41** (exact, no drop, post-gap) |
| Host replay | **41/41 verified** — `liveHash`, `pairCount`, `determinedCount`, dropped-edge mask all reproduce |
| Scored | **0** |
| Clears bar | **0** |

**Every one of the 41 returns `no-consistent-board`.** They do not fail the language bar; they
never reach it. `PostBombeDiscriminator.completedSteckers` finds **no ≤10-plug involution that
satisfies the whole menu** for any of them.

**This corrects Phase 51.16.** That entry named the blocker as a missing *geometry-aware full
decrypt*, and the fix was scoped accordingly. The coordinate bug was real — post-gap geometry does
put every transmitted step after the gap δ higher, and the dense `decrypt` does index it wrongly —
but it was **never the binding constraint**. These candidates die one stage earlier, at the plug
sieve.

**Why, and it is the oldest documented failure in this campaign.** The 41 trace to six source
Futures, all of them **multi-component**:

| Future | crib | edges | letters | components | loops |
|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 512 | `EINSCHIFFUNGHELA` | 16 | 20 | **5** | 1 |
| 525 | `FLUECHTLINGEANBORD` | 18 | 20 | **3** | 1 |
| 550 | `AUSLAUFENKIELNORWEGEN` | 21 | 20 | **2** | 3 |
| 602 | `SOFORTMELDENLAGE` | 16 | 19 | **4** | 1 |
| 605 | `SOFORTMELDENLAGE` | 16 | 18 | **3** | 1 |
| 623 | `SOFORTMELDENLAGE` | 16 | 19 | **3** | **0** |

This is Phase 4 exactly: a one-letter seed reaches **one** component and never tests the other two
to four, so a stop appears for structural reasons. Completion then demands every component admit a
consistent seed and the merge be a valid ≤10-plug involution, and the joint board contradicts.
Future 623 is a *tree* — zero loops, no redundancy at all.

**Independent corroboration, not just our own code agreeing with itself.** The campaign already
graded the sibling family through its own path (`PostBombeDiscriminator.rank`) and labelled all
**40** exact identity candidates `exact-identity-killed-by-completion` — also zero completions. Two
families, two code paths, same inventory, same verdict. So **all 81 exact candidates in that stripe
are split-menu ghosts**, and the adjudicator reproduces the campaign's outcome on the half the
campaign could already see.

**The actionable consequence is about inventory, not compute.** `--bombe-min-crib 16` admits these
menus because they are ≥16 letters, but length was never the right gate — **connectivity is**, and
that lesson is already printed in Phase 12 and Phase 4. A 16-letter crib spread over 20 letters in
5 components has less deduction power than a 16-letter crib in one. The next manifest builder
should gate on components and loops, which would have excluded all six of these Futures before a
single setting ran. More settings against weak menus manufactures ghosts; that is the whole lesson
of menu 627.

#### What this phase does **not** claim

No decrypt, key, or plaintext, and P1030680 remains unbroken. The verdict covers the **41
exact/no-drop post-gap candidates only**: they are eliminated as ghosts at the completion sieve.
The **4,103 repaired candidates remain ungraded here** — `isBreak` opens on `cribExact`,
which is undefined when the board discarded a letter. Phase 62 later consumed
`--mulein-ostwald-adapt` against the bounded stripe: 38 identity repairs climbed, **NO BREAK**;
post-gap dense climb stays illegal. Settings `256..<456976`, every other shell, the middle-ring gap, and the
printed legacy remainders are untouched by this work. The sparse scorer is now built and calibrated
but has graded **no** surviving candidate, because none survived; its value is that it proved where
the 41 actually die, and it is ready for any future candidate that clears completion. Arm E of the
calibration control shows an excluded edge produces a barrier, which is a mechanism check and
**not** a bar for repaired candidates.

#### What this phase does **not** claim

No decrypt, key, plaintext, or verdict on any candidate — nothing has been adjudicated and no code
has been written. Scoping to the 41 is **not** a claim that the 4,103 repaired hits are dead; it
is a statement that they have no declared bar yet. A graded 41 will most likely produce quarantine
material rather than a gate, and that is the expected outcome, not a disappointment. The
adjudicator changes how existing evidence is *read*; it adds no coverage and no settings.

### 56 — Constellation menus: joint anchors without an invented sentence

Phase 55.3 set the doctrine — Neustadt transfer and scuttling are linked stages, so
"let independently attested strings compete independently" and "do not concatenate them into an
invented sentence." That left a gap in the machinery. `NEUSTADT` is 8 letters with no
single-component placement, so the ledger correctly bars it from solo GPU; but a phrase can be
historically strong and mechanically unusable at the same time, and we had no way to let it
contribute.

A **constellation** closes that gap. It is one joint hypothesis over several independent anchors,
where only the absolute `(step, plaintext, ciphertext)` constraints are unioned. The anchors are
never concatenated and **no plaintext is asserted in the gap between them**. Mechanically this is
the Phase 4 split-menu lesson run forwards: two short anchors that are each weak alone can form a
single connected graph with real loops, provided the union is genuinely one component.

**What landed.** `BombeMenuBuilder.constellation` is fail-closed: a malformed, overlapping,
out-of-range or self-enciphering anchor rejects the *whole* constellation rather than silently
dropping one member and leaving a weaker menu that still looks like the plan. Endpoints are always
rebuilt from the root ciphertext; a fixture-supplied edge array is never trusted. The discriminator
followed: `cribExact` is now the conjunction over anchors, so a stop cannot pass by satisfying one
anchor and missing the other, and the tail statistic scores only physically contiguous uncovered
runs, so excising two anchors cannot invent an n-gram across the excision or across the gap.
Legacy one-anchor menus keep their historical statistic byte-for-byte. `menusAreIndependent`
became constraint-disjointness, which *tightens* `--bombe-confirm`: two hypotheses sharing even
one board constraint no longer count as independent confirmation.

**The staged plan.** `Fixtures/p1030680_wenselaers_procedure_neustadt_constellations.json`:
`FFFTTTBLEIBTBESETZT` × `NEUSTADT`, 24 rows, each **27 edges / 19–21 letters / 7–9 loops / one
component**, every anchor placement used at most twice. Both strings are repo-verbatim
(`FFFTTTBLEIBTBESETZT` in P1030668; `NEUSTADT` in P1030669 and P1030693 on two different nets).
Topology is cross-checked between the Python generator's union-find and the Swift board, and a
test fails loudly if either anchor ever stops being verbatim in `u534_corpus.json`.

**Priced, and the price changed the run order.** Preflight banner
(`logs/constellation-preflight-banner.log`): 24/24 selected, 34 944 shells/menu → 1.6×10¹⁰ settings
each, **3.83×10¹¹ total, ~2.1 h ETA floor and ~2.8 h at the measured 38M/s.** Affordable. But the
banner also printed the reason to wait:

> `residual gap: middle ring pinned A, so keys whose middle wheel turns over inside a 67-letter span (~12% for 1 notch, ~23% for 2) are not covered`

That is the finding. **Loops are not free.** A 27-edge contiguous crib spans 27 positions; these
constellations span **47–67**, because the anchors sit far apart. Span, not edge count, is what a
pinned middle ring fails to cover, so the wide joint menus are exactly the ones with the biggest
blind spot — up to **~23%** of key space against a few percent for a compact crib. Running them
ahead of the middle-ring arm would mean spending 2.8 h to search the rows we are least able to
clear. They therefore stay **behind** the full-middle arm, on the same reasoning that already put
the four-placement exact-radio context fixture behind it.

#### 56.1 — Arm COMPLETE: clean negative

Ran 2026-09-23, `logs/campaign-wenselaers-constellation-rings.log`:

```
total raw stops: 0 over 383245664256 settings (42.5M/s mean)
survived the 10-plug sieve: 0
no setting survived — every runnable menu contradicted everywhere it was tried
quarantine: 0 soft-band near-misses (solo pass complete)
```

24/24 menus dead at the board in 9,007 s. **Zero** raw stops is a stronger shape than the usual
"many stops, none physical": the board never even produced a candidate to sieve. No quarantine file
was written because nothing entered the soft band.

**What is eliminated:** under right-ring coverage with middle ring pinned A, no key places
`FFFTTTBLEIBTBESETZT` *and* `NEUSTADT` at any of the 24 tested alignments.
**What is not:** the Volksliste III history is untouched — a correct historical model can still
produce a signal that says this in words we have not guessed, or says something else. The test was
the narrow proposition that both strings sit verbatim in this ciphertext at those offsets.
**Residual:** the pinned middle ring leaves ~12–23% of keys untested across these 40–67 letter
spans. The swept version costs 5–7 days and is **not** the recommended next spend (Phase 56.2).

#### 56.2 — Unicity: the barrier is the estimator, not the information

Recorded because it changes the ranking of every remaining arm, and because the campaign had been
treating 72 letters as possibly hopeless without doing the arithmetic.

| Quantity | Value |
|---|---|
| Settings (wheels × greek × UKW × rings × positions) | 4.151×10¹¹ |
| Ten-plug boards, 26!/(6!·10!·2¹⁰) | 1.507×10¹⁴ |
| **Total key entropy** | ≈6.3×10²⁵ ≈ **2⁸⁶** (~86 bits) |
| German redundancy at ~3.2 bits/letter × 72 | ≈**230 bits** |
| **Unicity distance** | ≈86/3.2 ≈ **27 letters** |

We are at **~2.7× unicity distance.** Exactly one key yields German, by a margin of ~144 bits.
Therefore **every negative in this ledger is a search failure, not an information failure** — the
answer provably exists in the space we are enumerating.

Independent confirmation that the *estimator* is the binding constraint
(`Scripts/lm_margin_probe.py`, `logs/lm-margin-probe-20260923.log`): a from-scratch Python M4
(`Scripts/enigma_m4.py`) plus leave-one-out n-grams, grading truth against **genuine wrong-key
decrypts** with the plugboard hill-climbed symmetrically, reproduces the standing negative from a
completely separate implementation — median margin at 72 letters **−0.053** (n=4 best of orders
2–5), versus the Swift pipeline's −0.036…−0.123. Two independent paths agreeing on a negative is
much stronger than either alone.

**A methodology trap worth printing.** The first version of that probe reported **+1.14 margin and
100% wins at 72 letters** — an apparent overturning of the campaign's central negative. The bug was
handing the *true plugboard* to the truth and every decoy: with the real board the correct setting
decrypts to clean German and wrong settings are noise, so any scorer separates them. It was grading
with the answer key. Forcing the board to be climbed symmetrically at every candidate moved the
result from +1.14 to −0.044. That single correction *is* the difficulty of the problem, and it is
why exhaustion must always be applied to truth and decoys alike.

**Scope limit on that probe, stated because it under-tests the hypothesis:** it trained on 4,644
letters (leave-one-out over the naval corpus), while the repo's dense trigram table comes from
28.5M letters. So it measures *"order saturates at n=4 on 4.6k training letters"*, **not** "better
German models cannot help." The dense table already moved 72-letter recovery 4→21 letters, so the
training-data axis remains live and was held fixed here.

#### 56.3 — Marginalising over the plugboard: tried, measured, **does not work**

The estimator fix named in 56.2 was built and graded: replace the profile likelihood (max over
plugboards) with basin-shape proxies, on the theory that a true setting sits in a broad basin while
a ghost sits on a narrow spike. Three proxies, each computed identically for the truth and every
decoy: `ablation` (mean score after deleting each found plug), `swap` (mean over single-plug
perturbations), `logsumexp` (soft integral over the climb's accepted boards).
`Scripts/basin_margin_probe.py`, `logs/basin-margin-probe-20260923.log`.

**On margin it looked like the best lever the campaign has ever measured at 72 letters:**

| method | med margin | vs `peak` |
|---|---:|---|
| `peak` (current estimator) | −0.0394 | baseline |
| `ablation` | −0.0373 | better |
| **`swap`** | **−0.0202** | **~49% of the deficit removed** |
| `logsumexp` | −0.0280 | better |

That would have beaten the ledger's best 72-letter figure (depth-2 exhaustion, −0.0361). **It is
an artefact, and two checks killed it.**

1. **Compression, not separation.** The win rate did not move at all (17% for `peak`, `ablation`
   and `swap` alike) — the tell. Decoy standard deviation shrank in lockstep with the margin
   (0.0306 → 0.0212 for `swap`), i.e. the whole score distribution was squashed toward zero.
   Dividing every score by ten would "improve" every margin identically while separating nothing.
   The scale-free statistic *z* = (truth − decoy mean)/decoy sd is immune to that, and on the first
   12-control run `swap`'s *z* went the **wrong** way: `peak` −0.175 → `swap` **−0.489**.
2. **Seed instability.** A later 30-control run reported `swap` *z* = **+0.130**, contradicting
   run 1. Re-measuring across 5 independent seeds × 30 controls settles it: `swap` is
   **consistently worse** than `peak` (*z* −0.07…−0.33, never once better); `logsumexp`
   **straddles** `peak` (+0.06…+0.49 vs +0.01…+0.34), so its edge is noise; and `peak`'s own *z*
   swings **0.014…0.335** between seeds — the measurement is coarser than any effect claimed. A
   lever whose sign flips with the seed is not a lever.

**Two process lessons, printed because they generalise.** First, *margin alone is not sufficient
evidence* — it is the statistic this campaign quotes most often and it is rescaling-sensitive, so
the decoy-sd and *z* columns are now printed beside it and `basin_margin_probe.py` refuses to call
a margin gain progress unless *z* moves too. Second, *single-seed runs cannot support a claim at
this sample size*; the tool now takes `--seeds` and says so. Had only the margin table been
recorded, this would have entered the ledger as a 49% improvement and evaporated on re-run.

Marginalising over the nuisance parameter remains theoretically the right response to the
multiple-comparisons diagnosis in 56.2. What is refuted is that these three cheap basin proxies
implement it usefully. A genuine integral over the plugboard posterior is not what was tested.

#### 56.4 — More German text is not the lever; register-matched text is

56.2 flagged that the margin probe was confounded: it trained on **4,644 letters**, so it could not
separate "modelling is exhausted" from "the training set is tiny". That confound is now resolved,
and the answer is not the one expected.

**Corpus expansion, and a self-inflicted gap closed.** `Scripts/scrape_u534.py` collected two
categories and filtered page names to `^P\d{7}$` — which is precisely why the rest of the BGNC
project was invisible: the Norrköping intercepts are named `PAGE_23_COEW`, and the Graf Spee /
Rasch / Schroeder / Spanish messages have prose page names. BGNC states it has broken 70+ messages;
we were modelling register on 48. `Scripts/scrape_bgnc_wider.py` (new) collects the wider corpus
into a **separate** fixture — `u534_corpus.json` is load-bearing for campaign controls and the
verified `enigma_m4.py` round-trip, and was not perturbed.

**Validation first, because a scraped plaintext is a claim, not evidence.**
`Scripts/validate_bgnc_wider.py` requires equal-length cipher/plain pairs, **zero
self-encipherments** (Enigma's one absolute law — a single violation proves the two streams are not
a genuine pair), a legal plug involution, and German plaintext. Of 13 keyed records, 12 validate
(1,704 letters). Five carried a scrape artefact — one stray trailing capital swept from following
prose into both streams, showing up as a self-encipherment at exactly index *len−1* — repaired
**offline** by a proof-gated trim that is applied only when it yields zero self-encipherments, so a
genuinely bad pair can never be trimmed into looking valid. The Spanish Enigma message is a real
break but Spanish plaintext, and is **rejected** rather than allowed to poison a German model.

**The A/B, one variable changed** (`Scripts/lm_margin_corpus_ab.py`,
`logs/lm-margin-corpus-ab-20260923.log`). Identical controls, identical wrong-key positions per
seed, plugboard climbed symmetrically, order 4, 3 seeds:

| arm | training letters | mean margin | mean *z* | *z* range |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| A | 4,644 | −0.0482 | **+0.135** | [+0.129, +0.142] |
| B | 6,348 (+37%) | −0.0698 | **−0.527** | [−0.564, −0.468] |

**37% more text made it worse on both metrics, on every seed, with the *z* ranges fully disjoint.**
Δmargin −0.0216, Δ*z* −0.662. Not noise.

**Cause, and the lesson.** Ten of the twelve added messages are Norrköping **Swedish intercepts of
Luftwaffe traffic**: `TAETIGKEITSBERIQT`, `LFLXKDO` (Luftflottenkommando), `JAEGER` — a different
service, a different register, and different orthography (`Q` for `CH`, `SEQS` for `SECHS`). The
target is Kriegsmarine naval procedure. Diluting naval register with Luftwaffe reports degraded
discrimination, which is the same mechanism Phase 50.10 saw when naval specialisation *helped* only
where the attack already won.

So the hypothesis "a better language model breaks 72 letters" is now narrowed rather than refuted:
**volume of German is not the axis; fidelity to the target's register is.** Useful corollary for the
archival arm — 1,704 letters of *Kriegsmarine Baltic training* traffic would be a different
experiment from 1,704 letters of Luftwaffe traffic, and only the former is worth scraping for. This
also means the remaining BGNC material is not the resource it appeared to be: the M4 naval subset,
not the page count, is what matters.

No key, no plaintext, no decrypt. Credit: the breaks and transcriptions in that corpus are the
Breaking German Navy Ciphers project's work (Michael Hörenberg and collaborators); the fixture is a
research-input copy and any publication must credit BGNC.

**Priority consequence.** The plugboard is a **daily** setting, not per-message. A second Thetis
signal from 1 May would constrain the same 47-bit board with 144 letters instead of 72, and the
oracle ladder already shows **4 correct plugs flip the margin positive**. So archival discovery
outranks additional sweeping by a wide margin — including outranking the 5–7 day swept
constellation. The estimator route worth building is marginalising over the plugboard (basin
robustness) instead of maximising over it, which is the standard remedy for the nuisance-parameter
overfitting this probe measured.

#### 56.5 — Mining for levers: three human methods dead, one real prior found

After 56.3 and 56.4 closed the estimator and data routes, the remaining question was whether any
classical method or existing data had gone unexploited. Three were tested and are dead; one lever
is real.

**Cillies / operator key habits — dead, measured.** Bletchley's most productive human lever was
operator laziness in choosing message keys. Measured across the 47 recovered keys: **11/47** carry a
repeated letter against a uniform expectation of **10.2**; per-position letter frequencies are flat
(the most common letter at any position appears 4–6 times in 47, which is chance). No keyboard
walks, no repeats, no reuse. These operators randomised properly. There is no prior here, and that
is now a measurement rather than an assumption.

**Indicator / Grundstellung — mathematically dead.** Confirmed against the pre-existing
`Scripts/indicator_constraints.py`: message key = f(daily key, Grund) and Grund → message key is a
bijection, so substituting one unknown for the other reduces nothing. Having 48
(indicator, message-key) pairs does not help, because the Grund table that links them is lost and
recovering it would buy no reduction even if it were not.

**Banburismus — inapplicable.** It needs two messages in depth. The target has no partner; that is
the campaign's founding problem.

**The lever that is real: rotor VIII on the fast wheel.** Every daily key recovered from 1 May 1945
agrees, across three separate nets:

| net | messages | wheel order |
|---|---:|---|
| 438 / AACU | 31 | IV / III / **VIII** |
| 568 / AAEL | 16 | V / VI / **VIII** |
| 528 / AAFB | 1 | V / II / **VIII** |

Under independent per-net choice, (1/8)² ≈ **1.6%**. The campaign already carried a general
convention prior — `naval-two-notch-right`, "one of VI/VII/VIII on the fast wheel", 126 of 336
orders. This is tighter and grounded in *this* corpus rather than convention: **VIII specifically,
42 of 336 orders.** Measured effect on the parked middle-ring arm, read from the sweep banner:
113,568 shells/menu instead of 908,544 → 5.19×10¹⁰ settings, **ETA ~6.6 h instead of ~28 h**, with
`no residual ring gap for these menus` still printed so full middle+right coverage is preserved.

**It eliminates nothing, and the code says so in three places.** It reorders the search; the other
294 wheel orders remain open, and a negative under this prior is **not** wheel-order coverage.
Honest limits printed with the receipt: three nets is a thin sample; the nets may have drawn from a
shared Schlüsseltafel rather than choosing independently; and these are the keys that happened to be
broken rather than a random draw. **Whether 1 May allocation assigned fast wheels centrally across
nets is a historical question, not a mathematical one, and is referred to the archival arm** — if
central assignment is documented, this becomes a strong constraint on Thetis; if nets drew freely it
stays a weak prior. `--subspace viii-fast-wheel` · `Scripts/wheel_order_prior.py --check` recomputes
it and fails if the pattern ever breaks.

A real bug surfaced while wiring it: there are **two** subspace dispatchers
(`M4ThetisAttack.subspace(named:)` for the Welchman path, `p1030680CampaignSubspace` for the Mulein
path). Adding the case to only one produced a bare trace trap — exit 133, no output — via
`preconditionFailure` on the unknown name. Both now carry it and the error text lists the name.

#### What this phase does **not** claim

The mechanism ran and returned a clean negative; **P1030680 remains unbroken, no key, no plaintext,
no decrypt.** The VIII prior is an ordering heuristic, not evidence about Thetis's wheels; the
wider-corpus cribs are deliberately **not** run. A constellation is a menu-construction mechanism,
not evidence that these two phrases co-occur in this message — the historical case for that pairing is Selm's
reported Volksliste III process and stays at source-reported grade. Single-component and
`≤10`-plug completion still govern; nothing here weakens the ghost sieve, and the independence
change makes confirmation stricter, not easier. The 24 rows are a *plan*, and a plan that has
evaluated zero settings eliminates nothing.

### 57 — Next phase: the archival object that would change the mechanics

A planning entry written while the VIII-fast middle-ring arm was still running. The arm is now
**empty** (Phase 58): 24/24 dead, 0 raw stops, 0 quarantine, VIII-fast subspace only. The ranking
below still holds — compute is not where the probability is — and the first GPU item it named is
now **DONE** (Phase 60): 4/4 dead, 0 stops.

#### 57.1 — What Phase 56 leaves us with

Five hypotheses closed in one day, and the pattern across them is the finding:

| Route | Verdict |
|---|---|
| Constellation pairing (Selm's Neustadt/BLEIBTBESETZT) | clean negative, 0 stops / 3.83×10¹¹ |
| Basin/marginalisation estimator | refuted — compression, not separation; seed-unstable |
| Corpus volume (+37% text) | refuted — *worse*, register fidelity is the axis |
| Cillies / operator key habits | dead — 11/47 repeats vs 10.2 expected, keys are random |
| Indicator / Grundstellung | dead — bijection, buys exactly nothing |

Against one real lever (**VIII-fast**, ordering only) and one reframe (**unicity**: the plaintext
*is* determined, so this is a search failure). Every closed route was a *mechanical* route. The
ranking that falls out is not a preference, it is arithmetic: the plugboard is a **daily** setting,
4 correct plugs flip the margin positive, and a second same-day message constrains the same 47-bit
board with 144 letters instead of 72. **No sweep we can run competes with that.**

#### 57.2 — The one archival object with a mechanical payoff we can name

Selm has offered physical archive work (Cuxhaven or elsewhere) and asked for a specific reference
rather than a wish list. Phase 56.5 produced exactly one, and it is the first time this campaign can
name a *document* whose content would change the *search space* rather than merely add a crib:

> **The 1 May 1945 Kriegsmarine Schlüsseltafel / Zuteilungsliste — specifically whether wheel
> orders were assigned centrally across nets, or drawn independently per net.**

Why it matters mechanically, stated so the request is justified rather than hopeful:

* All three recovered nets that day put rotor **VIII** in the fast position (p ≈ 1.6% under
  independence). We are currently forced to treat that as an **ordering prior only** — 42 of 336
  orders run first, the other 294 stay open, and no negative under it counts as coverage.
* If central assignment is **documented**, the prior becomes a **constraint**: 42 orders is then the
  space, not the head of a queue, and the middle-ring arm becomes a genuine 8× *elimination* rather
  than a reordering. Every subsequent arm inherits that factor.
* If nets drew **independently**, VIII-fast stays a weak hint and we keep paying full price. Either
  answer is worth having; only an archive can give it.

This is the correct shape for an archival ask because it is **falsifiable from a single document**
and its two outcomes lead to different engineering, which is not true of "more cribs please."

Ranked below it, unchanged in priority but now better argued: **any independently identifiable
same-period Thetis transmission** (a second message constrains the same daily plugboard);
Bauer **Anhang F** when the page exists (Hydra May 1945 *stecker* control, not this
ciphertext's board, and not an VIII-fast answer unless the page also prints Walzenlage);
then exact ID / raw stream for `KLAR ZUM VERSENKEN GEM. BEFEHL.`; then the DEFE page images;
then the later allocation list’s date / codename / provenance.

#### 57.3 — Selm's 2026-09-23 tranche, graded

Received: a consolidated U-3521 source dossier (183 pp.) with **photographs of surviving KTB pages**
rather than forum transcriptions, plus a two-page research/evidence guide. Her own grading is
adopted, including her refusals. Public copy, credited to her:
[`site/public/selm/u3521-source-dossier-revised.pdf`](site/public/selm/u3521-source-dossier-revised.pdf).
The journal quotes her cover guide directly:

> The pages below are useful for reconstructing U-3521's training and final-days context. They do not establish that U-3521 used M-Thetis, and no raw Thetis Funkspruch or Thetis key has been identified in this dossier.

> The KTB material supports the 2 May sequence "crew leaves except Sprengkommando" and movement by T-boat to Neustadt. It does not itself state that this crew movement was to the 3. U-Lehrdivision.

| Report | Grade | Note |
|---|---|---|
| U-3521 begins Agru-Frontausbildung 2 April; operates under Agru-Front Befehl | **source-reported, photographed** | Upgrade from forum transcription |
| 9 April KTB: `Auf Sehrohrtiefe (FT-Programmzeit)` | **source-reported, photographed** | Scheduled broadcast reception during Baltic training — the concrete form of the FT-Programm layer |
| 30 Apr / 1 May `Vorbereitungen zur Versenkung des Bootes` | **source-reported, photographed** | |
| 2 May `Besatzung verläßt Boot, bis auf Sprengkommando. Fährt mit T-boot nach Neustadt.` | **source-reported, photographed** | |
| 3 May `Sprengkommando von Bord / Boot versenkt` | **source-reported, photographed** | |
| **No second Thetis transmission in the dossier** | **her own explicit negative** | Recorded as she stated it; she declined to oversell |
| DEFE 3/579 ZTPG 369048 (1 May) places U-3014, U-2538, U-3024 together at Travemünde | **source-reported, database reference** | Page images still being pursued |

**The evidential distinction she insisted on is adopted verbatim and must not be collapsed.** The
KTB documents the crew going **to Neustadt**; it does **not** say *3. U-Lehrdivision*. That link
comes only from the separate Volksliste III order (ZTPG 367835). Two sources, two claims, kept
separate. Consequently: **this is procedural/contextual evidence, not evidence that U-3521 itself
used Thetis.**

**A trap this tranche creates, flagged before anyone walks into it.** ZTPG 369048 co-locating
U-3014, U-2538 and U-3024 at Travemünde is *tempting* corroboration for the boat-number cribs — and
it does **not** resurrect them. The Phase 55 withdrawal rested on raw P1030704/P1030707 not carrying
`UUUDREINULZWOVIER` / `UUUZWOFUENFDREIACHT` cleanly, which is a **transcription** fact the DEFE
material does not touch. Boats being in the same harbour is not a letter string in this ciphertext.
The withdrawal stands.

Her open enquiries: Hörenberg (the 1 May 23:56 Wogram/Musketier message carrying
`Klar zum Versenken gem. Befehl.` — underlying P1030xxx number, scan filename, original
Schlüsselzettel, raw pre-normalisation stream, indicator groups, net/key; then the 1945
Zuteilungsliste's validity period and M-Thetis Kenngruppen allocation, and the DEFE 3/579
provenance/access route) and Dean Paton / Big Heritage (surviving U-534 radio material 30 April–2
May, P1030680-related material, possible duplicates, Thetis documentation). No reply is evidence
until it arrives.

#### 57.4 — What runs if the VIII-fast arm comes up empty

**The arm came up empty (Phase 58).** In order, unchanged except that item 2 is now done:

1. **Report and ask (no compute).** Send the completed arm, the five closed routes, and the

#### What this phase does **not** claim

No decrypt, key, or plaintext; **P1030680 remains unbroken.** The U-3521 material is procedural
context photographed from a private KTB, **not** evidence that U-3521 used Thetis and **not** a

### 58 — VIII-fast middle ring COMPLETE: clean negative, and why not Mulein-Girard

The resume arm finished. `logs/campaign-middlering-viii-fast-resume2.log`:

| Slice | Coverage | Result |
|---|---|---|
| 1/24 (earlier) | full 336 wheel orders, middle × right | dead over 4.152×10¹¹ settings |
| 2–24 | `--subspace viii-fast-wheel` (42 WO), middle × right, `no residual ring gap` | **23/23 dead at the board, 0 raw stops over 1.194×10¹² settings** @ 72.1M/s, 0 physical, **0 quarantine** |

Every runnable menu contradicted everywhere it was tried. The FLOTT-family head (19–20 loops,
one component) died the same way as the diversity tail (5–8 loops, often split). **P1030680
remains unbroken; this is not wheel-order coverage**; the other 294 orders stay open.

#### 58.1 — Do not encode Girard as a weighted Mulein deletion board

The tempting next engine is "Mulein-Girard": keep the tolerant board, but charge less to delete
edges whose letters sit in Girard's named confusion classes (U/N, Q/G, H/F). Three independent
receipts say that is the wrong object:

1. **It is a subset of uniform tolerance 1.** Uniform *t*=1 already enumerates *every* one-edge
   deletion. Girard-weighted *t*=1 can only accept a subset of those survivors. The strongest
   three placements are already **dead at uniform *t*=1 × right rings** (Phase 51.12). A cheaper
   cost on a named subset cannot revive a stop that full *t*=1 rejected at the same shells.
2. **Phase 52.4 already measured the neighbourhood.** Menus that contradict everywhere at
   tolerance 0 project **0** stops at tolerance 1: there is nothing for a weighted deletion to
   widen. The VIII-fast exact arm just printed that shape at full middle-ring coverage.
3. **Girard's named pairs are not the measured errors.** `Scripts/girard_garble_classes.py`
   against P1030681's re-checked transcripts: 44 substitution events, top pairs **L↔R (4),
   H↔S (3)**; F↔H appears **once**, and U/N and Q/G do not appear in the top twenty. Building
   the labels he spoke while re-reading would be cargo-culting the names, not the method.
4. **His method was re-reading, not dropping.** Deletion *loses* a constraint (weaker board,
   more ghosts). Girard *replaced a ciphertext letter* and kept a 40-edge menu. Those are
   different hypotheses. If a garble tool is ever built, it is **transcript forks** (*g*≤1
   substitutions drawn from the *measured* distribution), control-first on P1030684, not a
   Metal cost function on `propagateTolerant`.
5. **The 72-letter prior is weak, not zero.** Same script: P1030680 is 18 groups. On
   P1030681, errors in the first 72 letters are **4 (sz) / 0 (pp) / 0 (final)**. The
   "disarranged after group 40" story never reaches this message. Single-copy error remains
   possible — P1030680 has no second transcript — but it is not the sheet-tail phenomenon,
   and Phase 50.9 already withdrew "16 controls prove archive garble" as too thin to fit a
   confusion model.

Uniform *t*=1 × VIII-fast × middle ring on the FLOTT head was never run, and it is still the
wrong spend: 40× a 6.6 h arm, on menus whose *t*=0 neighbourhood is empty.

#### 58.2 — What to build instead

Host-side, no new settings: an adapter from the 4,103 Nazi Blaster one-edge repairs into
`--ostwald-escalate`. **Built in Phase 59, consumed in Phases 62–64:** 38 then 824 then
4,101 identity/gapped-post-gap repairs climbed, **NO BREAK**. Metal Ostwald is the Phase 63
climber (4.6M decrypts/s). Phase 64 included the gapped post-gap walk. The 2026 Ostwald path
(ranker / beam / no-IC-sieve all-settings) is Phase 65.

GPU, hours: the BLEIBTBESETZT 40-letter context fixture, VIII-fast, middle ring
unpinned (57.4 item 3) — **DONE (Phase 60)**, 4/4 dead, 0 stops.

#### What this phase does **not** claim

No decrypt, key, or plaintext. A negative under VIII-fast is **not** a negative on the other
294 wheel orders and **not** a proof the ciphertext is ungarbled. Not building Mulein-Girard
does not close the sister-message garble hypothesis; it refuses a mechanism that cannot add
coverage the campaign has already paid for.

### 59 — Identity-repair → Ostwald adapter, built; stripe not consumed

`--mulein-ostwald-adapt` streams a campaign JSONL, rebounds the Future inventory **by content**
(SHA-256 + fingerprint), and compiles **only the identity-repair ordinals** that appear on
candidate rows. That is the fast path: it is not a rematerialize of all 628 Futures and not a
TensorLUT rerun. Forced plugs are rebuilt from the host `live` bitmask. Split menus
(`comp≠1`), pairCount < 4, and duplicate shell+live-hash rows are counted into a skip JSONL
with an explicit reason.

Dense `--ostwald-escalate` is legal only for **identity-family** repairs (same 72-letter
recorded timeline). Post-gap repairs are skipped as `post-gap-dense-ostwald-illegal` — after a
hole the 72-letter walker enciphers every later symbol at a rotor position δ too low, and 82%
of post-gap hits in the stripe are identity closures at `lane − 4`. Replacement Futures were
never inventoried; one-edge repair is not a `.replacement` fork.

```bash
./.build/release/helut --mulein-ostwald-adapt \
  logs/p1030680-mulein-operational-settings-000001-000256-v3.jsonl
# then, if you want the climb:
./.build/release/helut --ostwald-escalate \
  logs/p1030680-mulein-identity-repair-ostwald.json
```

**Not run against the 1 GB stripe in this phase.** Phase 62 later consumed the adapter:
38 identity repairs climbed, NO BREAK. XCTest now covers both `--mulein-ostwald-adapt` and `--ostwald-escalate`
on synthetic P1030680-labeled fixtures and the P1030684 known-key control: skip reasons,
fail-closed rebound, known-key identity-repair emit, quarantine→escalate decode, crib-exact
on eight true plugs, and a ghost that does not clear. Tests do **not** load the 628-Future
operational JSON or the 1 GB stripe.

#### What this phase does **not** claim

No decrypt, key, or plaintext. Building the adapter does not grade the 2,056 identity repairs
or the 2,047 post-gap repairs. Crib-exact on a dropped letter remains undefined; the climber's

**source-reported structure inside naval key-sheet generation**. It is not yet an answer to
the cross-net question, and it is not a reason to promote VIII-fast to an elimination.

**NARA RG 457 / NSA Historic Cryptographic Collection — request acknowledged, no receipt.**
Formal request covering NR 201 (cipher book / key sheets 1944–45), NR 202 (key tables F, M, N
and emergency cipher), NR 205 (allocation lists for discriminants and call signs), NR 268
(indicator books), NR 301 (Navy indicator groups for “K” Book), NR 1665 Box 620

daily key, or enough allocation/key material to settle VIII-fast. A documented negative about
what survives is also a result. No box has been read.

short-wave traffic almost continuous; nearby boats interceptable; long-wave programme-time
repeats for missed serials; a boat may remain radio-silent but **must come up to listen to
routines**. That strengthens the already-OPEN radio-watch / not-addressed-to-U-534 reading of
the U-534 bundle. **The report never names Thetis.** AGRU-Front = Thetis stays rejected

### 61.1 — Tauschtafelplan Bruno: 1 May is Quelle / Tafel A (selection only)

Selm 2026-09-24. Scan of **Tauschtafelplan Bruno**, Prüfnr. **1772a**, Kennwort **Quelle**
(published Hörenberg copy). Printed heading *Tritt erst auf besonderen Befehl in Kraft*.
Column sechs is annotated **Mai 45**. Day **1** of that column is a clean printed **A**.

That is documentary table *selection* for P1030680:

`VROL NMKA → Quelle/A → EACH/SEDM → ACH → K-Buch 645/14`

The detector in `selm-detector/` now marks that Tauschtafel assignment **DIRECT** with
validity **1945-05-01 only**. Pair contents of Tafel A are now photographed in
Prüfnr. **2499** (Phase 61.3); 1772a vs 2499 edition compatibility is not
demonstrated. The surviving `621–653 = M-Thetis` crop stays **RECONSTRUCTED**
and undated, so `discovery_eligible` remains false and M-Thetis is **not** promoted.
P1030680 stays hard-excluded from discovery.

**3 May is not A by inheritance.** Day 3 of the same May column carries a handwritten mark.
It is not encoded. A 3 May classify against this package must fail closed. XCTest-equivalent:
`python -m unittest -v test_detector.py` (6 tests, including `test_may3_does_not_inherit_1_may_tafel_a`).

annotation is the evidence of use.

### 61.2 — Bauer *Ultra versus Enigma*: Hydra Appendix F cited, page not in hand

Selm 2026-09-24. Chapter scans of Friedrich O. Bauer, *Ultra versus Enigma* (pp. 31–41, 46,
48) and *Enigmafunk* (pp. 169–179). Graded against the attached pages, not against a
remembered appendix.

**Changeover (p. 35).** Inner setting (Walzenlage and Ringstellung) at midnight; outer
setting (Steckerverbindungen) at 12:00 noon. From **1.7.1942** the change is described as
simultaneous at noon. Deters reports that in practice the inner setting was still done by
the Funker (Funkmaat / Oberfunkmaat); U-boat operations had their own rules. Tenholt
(U 431, Mediterranean) is printed as a contradicting witness. This is Kriegsmarine
procedure, not a Thetis key.

**Tagesschlüssel survival (p. 36).** M.Dv. Nr. 32/3 Ziffer 59: destroy the daily key after
use. Almost no Tagesschlüssel papers survive. A 1980s Dutch find of Kenngruppentafeln for
M.Dv. Nr. 98 is referred to Anhang G.

**Appendix F (p. 36 n. 15), source-reported.** One Tagesschlüssel parameter is the
Steckerverbindungen. Bauer says those for May 1945 are to be taken from **Anhang F**, and
that struck-through positions show the instruction was in use through **7 May 1945**
inclusive. That is a surviving May-1945 daily-key *fragment* for **Schlüssel M Hydra**
(Heimische Gewässer, p. 34), not an illustrative blank. The appendix page is **not** in
the 13-page extract. Until that page is read: no Hydra 1 May board is in hand, and none
will be applied to P1030680.

**What Appendix F cannot do, as cited.** VIII-fast is a *Walzenlage* question (central
assignment vs independent nets). A Hydra *stecker* column answers a different parameter.
Hydra is a comparison/control net; Potsdam/Plaice daily keys are already eliminated by
exhaustion (C11). Importing Hydra plugs onto this ciphertext would be the plugboard form
of the imported-register wedge.

**1 May Grundstellung plurality (p. 34 n. 8), source-reported, not confirmed.** Bauer notes
a planned 1 May 1945 change: several Enigma-Grundstellungen per day instead of one
([34, p. 853], [50, p. 121]). The campaign already records the printed Grund table as gone.
If that change took effect it does not reopen the indicator attack, which remains a
bijection (Phase 56.5). It is a reason to want the actual Schlüsseltafel, not a crib.

**Enigmafunk (pp. 169–179).** Confirms Kenngruppen identical at start and end of a normal
Schlüssel-M message; from 1.7.1944 an extra three-letter group names the net (e.g. M Hydra
vs M Triton). No Thetis key. Table C's Funkschaltung **Bruno** is a radio-circuit label
(any area Atlantic or Indian Ocean), not Tauschtafelplan Bruno Prüfnr. 1772a. Kurzsignal /
WW weather forms are a different message class from P1030680's 72-letter body.

#### What this update does **not** claim

Not a decrypt. Not a Thetis Tagesschlüssel. Not a photographed Appendix F. Not a Hydra→Thetis
stecker transfer. Not an VIII-fast elimination. Not a confirmed 1 May multi-Grundstellung
sheet. P1030680 remains unbroken.

### 65 — 2026 Ostwald path built (ranker, beam, no-IC-sieve); curve is the gate

The 72-letter wall is the *score*, not FLOPs. Phase 65 keeps Ostwald's loop and replaces
what it maximises.

- `--ostwald-scorer ranker`: leave-one-out linear discriminant on IC, bigrams, trigrams,
  naval trigrams, entropy, digit-words, triples, XX, fitted against **wrong-setting
  decrypts** (not uniform random). P1030680 is not in the training set.
- `--ostwald-beam N`: keep N partial boards per insertion round (beam 1 = old greedy).
- `--ostwald-keep 0`: unlock all forced plugs (the previous ternary treated 0 as "keep
  all"). Flag absent still holds every forced plug.
- `--ostwald-all-settings`: climb every 26⁴ message key on one locked shell. Drops the IC
  sieve that ranked the true P1030684 key 223,118 / 456,976. Requires `--ostwald-control
  p1030684` or `--ostwald-shell` plus a quarantine ciphertext. One shell ≈ 8.2×10⁸
  decrypts, a few minutes on Metal.

This is not a language-model crib and is not pointed at P1030680 until
`--ostwald-curve --ostwald-scorer ranker` greets at 72 letters.

**Smoke (2026-09-24, exit 0):** `--ostwald-lengths 72 --ostwald-controls 8 --ostwald-wrong 8`.
6 of the 8 shortest round-tripping controls are ≥72 letters. **2/6 wins (33%), median
margin −1.0269** (ranker units — not comparable to staged trigram margins), median z 0.97,
median 1/10 true plugs, 8% letters correct. Not a zero crossing. Log:
`logs/ostwald-curve-ranker-smoke.log`.

**Full 72-letter curve (2026-09-24, exit 0, 5.7 s):** `--ostwald-lengths 72` on all
round-tripping controls, 16 wrong samples. Preflight 32/48. Eligible **30**.
**5/30 wins (17%), median margin −1.2000**, median z 0.59, 1/10 plugs, 7% letters.
The smoke overstated the win rate. Not a zero crossing. Not pointed at P1030680.
Log: `logs/ostwald-curve-ranker-72.log`.

**Decoy-climb fit (2026-09-24, exit 0, 6.0 s):** same curve plus
`--ostwald-ranker-decoy-climbs 1` (one staged ghost climb per training control).
**1/30 wins (3%), median margin −1.7306**, z 0.44, 0/10 plugs. Worse than identity
decrypts as the negative class — climbed ghosts share the statistic the truth is
trying to own. Log: `logs/ostwald-curve-ranker-decoy.log`.

**Beam 4 (2026-09-24, exit 0, 11.7 s):** `--ostwald-beam 4` on the same ranker.
**6/30 wins (20%), median margin −1.0925**, z 0.93, 1/10 plugs, 8% letters.
A slightly wider search does not flip the median. Log:
`logs/ostwald-curve-ranker-beam4.log`.

```bash
./.build/release/helut --ostwald-curve --ostwald-scorer ranker --ostwald-lengths 72
./.build/release/helut --ostwald-curve --ostwald-scorer ranker --ostwald-lengths 72 \
  --ostwald-ranker-decoy-climbs 1
./.build/release/helut --ostwald-all-settings --ostwald-control p1030684 \
  --ostwald-setting-count 64
```

#### What this phase does **not** claim

No decrypt. No 72-letter zero-crossing until the curve prints one. TensorLUT involution
search remains parallel research, not a Thetis crib (H6, N7).

### 66 — 4-plug hot-letter brute graded; frequency does not hold four true plugs

Phase 50.6: four *correct* plugs flip the 72-letter sign. `--ostwald-brute-plugs 4` was
already built and refused at exhaust 6 because four pairs need eight letters. Wired into
`--ostwald-curve` (default exhaust 8 when brute-plugs is 4; ABORT below 8).

**Hot-letter cell (2026-09-24, exit 0, 26.9 s):**
`--ostwald-curve --ostwald-scorer staged --ostwald-brute-plugs 4 --ostwald-exhaust 8
--ostwald-lengths 72`. 105 perfect matchings, applied symmetrically. Preflight 32/48,
30 eligible. **Oracle cover 0/32** — no round-tripped control has ≥4 true plugs inside
the 8 hottest ciphertext letters. Curve **1/30 wins (3%), median margin −0.1633**, z 0.19,
0/10 plugs, 6% letters. Worse than unseeded staged (~20% / −0.23 on the older 30-control
cell) because 105 extra decoy starts lift the ghost maximum. Log:
`logs/ostwald-curve-brute4-e8.log`.

**Unclimbed 4-plug probe (Metal 164M filter question):** `--ostwald-brute-probe`.
C(10,4)=210 true 4-subsets vs 2048 random 4-plug boards, trigram, same rotor setting,
12 round-tripped controls. **trueMax > rndMax on 8/12**. Several keys have 0/2048 randoms
above the true 4-subset (z up to 6.46); others have 1–24/2048 (P1030713). Scaling 24/2048
to 164 million 4-plug boards is ~1.9 million ghosts scoring higher — so a Metal *score*
of every 4-plug board does not uniquely surface the Phase 50.6 seed. Climbing all 164
million remaining-six boards is ~2 h/setting at 10M decrypts/s and is still symmetric
on decoys. Log: `logs/ostwald-brute-probe.log`.

```bash
./.build/release/helut --ostwald-brute-probe
```

Not a decrypt. Not a 164 million 4-plug Metal consume. Not an VIII-fast elimination.

### 67 — Streamed 4-plug alphabet climb: known-key greeting DONE

Phase 50.6 still says four *correct* plugs on the *true* rotor setting flip the
72-letter sign. Phase 66 showed ciphertext frequency does not contain those four,
and that *scoring* all 164 million 4-plug boards does not uniquely surface them.
The remaining mechanical brute is: posit every 4-plug board, climb the remaining
six, keep crib-exact / IC.

**Combinatorics.** C(26,8)×105 = **164,038,875** four-plug boards. Climb remaining
six from a 4-plug seed is **671** decrypts including CPU replacement
(`OstwaldProgress.greedyDecrypts(seeded: 4)`). Metal greedy insertion is **503**
(`greedyInsertDecrypts`). Streamed greeting scores the 503; polish-top 256 does
the replacement only on wave leaders. Product **8.25×10¹⁰ Metal decrypts per
locked setting**. Among those 164M are C(10,4)=210 true 4-subsets of a 10-plug
board — on the true setting those 210 should BREAK. On a wrong setting the same
budget produces stronger ghosts; Phase 64 already reached IC 0.0544 from 3.33
million climbs without this brute. IC/trigram at 72 letters cannot pick truth
from that tail (C11 ranked the true P1030684 key 223,118/456,976 on the IC sieve).
So "investigate the ghosts" without crib-exact does not eventually find the four
real plugs: it finds four plugs that look good on a wrong setting.

**Odds (what this greeting is, and is not).** This is not a lottery at 210/164M.
Those 210 true 4-subsets of a 10-plug board sit in the 164,038,875 starts at
about **1 in 781,000**. Ciphertext frequency cannot find them (Phase 66: 0/32).
The run visits every start, so on the *true* rotor setting every one of those
210 is climbed. What remained empirical was whether *greedy insertion* from them
(and replacement polish only on each wave's top 256 scores) still prints
crib-exact on this 72-letter text. Phase 50.6 froze four *oracle-correct*
plugs on a ten-message panel: 50% win, median +0.33. That is a different
experiment — message-to-message, plugs immovable. P1030684 is a key this
engine already recovers with Welchman and with eight oracle plugs; this
greeting asked whether an empty board plus 4-plug enumeration recovers that
same decrypt when the rotors are already right.

**Result (true setting, 2026-09-24T17:29:14Z, exit 0).** Restart
2026-09-24T16:51:24Z, 524,288-job waves, `--ostwald-memory-gb 56`, 313/313
waves, wall **~37.8 min @ 36.3M/s**. **1 candidate clears the break bar:**
`B/gamma/IV-III-VIII` rings AACU pos **VYAA**, crib **ok**, IC **0.0657**,
tail **−2.9671**, **10 pairs**,
`VVVUUUVIRSOBENNULEINSXXMITUUUVIRSIBENNULZWOYVIRSIBENNULDREIYZWODREISECHS`
— the known 72-letter P1030684 control window. Log
`logs/ostwald-fourplug-p1030684-true-385320.log`. This is a **known-key
greeting**, not a P1030680 decrypt.

**Plan (consume order).**

1. **DONE — true P1030684 setting.** VYAA = index **385320**.
2. **DONE — wrong P1030684 setting.** Index 0 (AAAA). Same budget.
   **crib BAD**, IC 0.0509, tail −3.0462, **NO BREAK**. Log
   `logs/ostwald-fourplug-p1030684-wrong-0.log`.
3. **Eligible, CLI wired, not launched as a lottery:** `--ostwald-control p1030680`
   `--ostwald-setting-from 0 --ostwald-setting-count 1` on the working-prior
   shell `B/beta/IV-III-VIII/AAAA` (override with `--ostwald-shell`). Empty
   cribs cannot clear the break bar (`OstwaldBreakBar.minAttestedCrib` = 16).
   Never 26⁴, never the 30-control curve, never
   `--ostwald-brute-settings-ok` as a way around the greeting. Phase 68 spends

```bash
./.build/release/helut --ostwald-all-settings --ostwald-control p1030684 \
```

#### What this phase does **not** claim

Not a P1030680 decrypt, key, or plaintext. The break-bar print is the **P1030684
known-key control**. Not a unique 72-letter IC filter. Not 26⁴ × 164M.

### 69 — Plugboard marginal, thermodynamic integral: compression only

The untested estimator from Phase 56.3 was built: Metropolis–Hastings on legal
≤10-plug involutions, tempered from β=1 to β=0, trapezoid of E_β[log L]. The
prior volume cancels, so the integral ranks the marginal. Truth and decoys walk
the same schedule. No record plugboard is loaded. Script
`Scripts/plugboard_marginal_probe.py`. Log
`logs/plugboard-marginal-probe-20260924.log`.

**8 clean-key controls, 6 same-shell wrong message keys, 3 seeds, order-4
leave-one-out, 72 letters, 24 MH steps × 7 temperatures.** Every seed:
margin less negative than the greedy peak, **z worse**, win rate 25% then 12%
then 12% against the peak's 12%. Verdict on every seed: **COMPRESSION ONLY**.
Same failure as the Phase 56.3 proxies. Not a filter. Not pointed at P1030680.

Thetis-register menus 15 and 16 each produced one physical stop (IC 0.049, tails
−5.028 and −5.804). Both tails miss the soft quarantine floor (−4.0), and the
sweep does not record the shell, so there is no Ostwald job to run.

#### What this phase does **not** claim

Not a decrypt. Not a better 72-letter estimator.

### 70 — Schlüssel M procedure in hand; FORT is not a runnable crib

Selm Merel Wenselaers recovered the Hellschreiber scan
`site/public/selm/article-antiUbootRDF-AOB-97.pdf` and read it against
M.Dv.Nr. 32/1 as reproduced by Bauer. She reports Appendix E (pp. 299–313) as
*Der Schlüssel M – Verfahren M Allgemein*, Appendix F (p. 314) as the May 1945
Hydra key sheet (Schl. T. M 4118), and Appendix G (p. 315) as
*Verschlüsseln und Entschlüsseln – Tafel D*, Kennwort Quelle, Prüf-Nr. 2409.
Public link: `/selm/article-antiUbootRDF-AOB-97.pdf`.

**What her reading adds, graded as her report of the manual, not as a decrypt.**

The Kenngruppen chain in §§42–45: a Schlüsselkenngruppe identifies the Schlüssel
and its Schlüsselbereich; a Verfahrenkenngruppe identifies Verfahren M Allgemein
and is typed, under the Tagesschlüssel, to produce the Spruchschlüssel. Buchkenngruppen
from the K-Buch are then encrypted under a separate Schlüsselverfahren für
Kenngruppen and sent as Funkkenngruppen. §45 places the two encrypted groups at
the start and repeats them at the end. That is why `VROL NMKA` must not be read
as the plaintext word Thetis. Tafel D is **not** applied to those groups.

The document to hunt is M.Dv.Nr. 98: K-Buch, Schlüsselheft für Kenngruppen,
Schlüsselkenngruppenzettel. A Marinenachrichtenauffangstelle named Thetis is a
separate question from Schlüsselbereich M-Thetis. She does not equate them.

**FORT (§§36–39).** The manual inserts `FORT` in the last third of every part
except the last, and in the first third of every part except the first, when a
message is split. `FORT` is four letters. `OstwaldBreakBar.minAttestedCrib` is
16. A four-letter crib cannot clear the break bar, and it is not in the crib
catalog as its own menu. The corpus hit `FAHRT NACH OSTEN FORT` is ordinary
German, not this marker. Seventy-two letters is 18 groups. The manual’s figure
of about 80 groups is a maximum, so the length does not show that P1030680 is a
Teilfunkspruch. No GPU job is queued. Sister parts, if found, are a historical
search: parts need not be adjacent.

**Not this phase:** loading Hydra stecker, applying Tafel D, equating the
Auffangstelle with M-Thetis, a `FORT` sweep, stopping the Thetis-register ring
sweep.

#### What this phase does **not** claim

Not a decrypt. Not a Kenngruppen solution. Not a new crib. Not a change to the
running Welchman sweep.

   * The scored-completion count moved **0 → 35**, and that is a *scoring-surface* change,

     and quarantine's soft IC floor (Phase 22). With a prefix rescue and a 0.048 gate, 35
     boards get scored; under Phase 8's strict whole-message 0.055 gate with no prefix
     reading, none did. The raw-stop count is the coverage-sensitive number, and it is
     identical.
   * **The archived `logs/campaign-top30-rings.log` is truncated at menu 6 of 30** and
     contains no summary at all — no stop total, no sieve count, no verdict. The 73 /
     0 physical / 4.79×10¹¹ / 37.8M/s figures in the Phase 8 write-up were never on disk.
     Same buffered-`tee` truncation as the Phase 10 resume log. The re-run is now the first
     on-disk receipt for this arm, and it independently corroborates the two
     coverage-sensitive figures. `logs/rerun-top30-rings-maxupper-2026-08-16.log`.
3. **DEPRIORITISED — the remaining 300 catalog placements** × right rings, ~32 h.
   Two independent arms at the *maximum* contamination share (5.56%) have now re-run with
   byte-identical raw stop counts and zero additional survivors. The prior that the cap was
   concealing a key in the remaining contaminated lanes is correspondingly weak, and a third
   right-ring pass buys little. The strongest of these placements are instead folded into
   item 8 at *full* ring coverage, which subsumes the repair and adds new key space.
   Fixture retained: `Fixtures/p1030680_maxupper_rerun_menus.json`.
4. Fuzzed arms (41 + 8 placements) — × right rings, ~5.3 h. Lowest share, lowest priority.

Note rings AAAA is *not* the right re-run for these: the sweep's own banner reports AAAA as
covering 0/26 of the ring space at ≥26 letters, so the AAAA pass never carried a meaningful
negative for a 28–40 letter menu. Right-ring coverage is the claim that has to be
re-established.

```bash
python3 Scripts/max_upper_audit.py Fixtures/p1030680_menus.json \
  --emit Fixtures/p1030680_maxupper_rerun_menus.json
./.build/release/helut --welchman \
  --bombe-fixture Fixtures/p1030680_uebung_body_menus.json \
  --bombe-menus 0 --bombe-min-crib 16 --bombe-ring-sweep \
  --bombe-quarantine logs/quarantine_maxupper_rerun.json
```

**11. TEAM ACTIVE — Wenselaers historical/archival arm.** See Phases 52 and 55.
Selm Merel Wenselaers is a deeply valued contributor and member of the campaign team, leading the
historical/archival arm. Her first three historically motivated phrases are closed where tested: exact and
post-gap δ=4 both give 28/28 dead, 0 raw stops over 4.471×10¹¹ settings each. Tolerance 1 remains
technically affordable but is historically deprioritised.

Her new tranche changes the model without authorising compute:

1. **Exact radio phrase elevated:** `FFFTTTBLEIBTBESETZT` is verbatim P1030668, independent of
   the corrupted P1030707 sister. The 19-letter core is mechanically weak, so a finite fixture uses
   the exact 40-letter source window `UHRJKIELWEINGELAUFENYFFFTTTBLEIBTBESETZT`: four
   alignment-diagonal representatives, each 18 loops / one component. **DONE** under
   VIII-fast × middle ring (Phase 60): 4/4 dead, 0 raw stops over 2.076×10¹¹ settings.
2. **Historical process linked:** Neustadt crew transfer and scuttling/demolition parties are
   reported as stages of one Volksliste III process, not competing scenarios. Keep their strings
   independent; do not invent a sentence joining them.
3. **Recipient opened:** preservation among U-534 papers does not establish intended recipient.
   Addressing/boat-number material or a short intercepted follow-up is an OPEN prior. The proposed
   clean U-3024/U-2538 strings are nevertheless **withdrawn** after raw P1030704/P1030707 recheck.
4. **Thetis chronology qualified:** changing Zuteilungslisten plausibly reconciles column 645's
   Aegir/Thetis mappings; exact later-list provenance remains pending. Working assignment stays
   M-Thetis, not an unqualified chronology claim.
5. **Archive work pre-empts plausible German:** live lines now include NARA RG 457 key logs /
   allocation lists (request acknowledged, no box read), Bauer Anhang F (Hydra May 1945
   *stecker* page cited, not in hand — Phase 61.2), Cuxhaven Funkkladden, Alkmaar M.Dv.Nr.98,
   Cloots/Beckers for 3 May Kenngruppen, Big Heritage, DSM U-2540. A real source reply — especially
   a second Thetis witness, a 1 May key table, or an original 8-letter indicator — outranks GPU.
   VIII-fast stays an ordering prior until the cross-net Schlüsseltafel question is answered
   (Phase 61). Hydra plugs are a control, not this ciphertext's board. Do not mint cribs from
   the NID Baltic-training vocabulary or from Bauer's Kurzsignal examples.
6. **Nazi Blaster adjudication DONE for exact families; identity-repair Ostwald DONE (Phases 62–64).**
   All 81 exact candidates are split-menu ghosts at joint completion (Phase 53.6).
   Phase 62 climbed 38 single-component identity repairs, **NO BREAK**. Phase 63 Metal-climbed
   **824** identity-family ≥4-plug repairs including split-menu trees, **NO BREAK**. Phase 64
   CPU-climbed **4,101** identity plus gapped post-gap repairs, **NO BREAK**, best −3.0390
   crib BAD (the +0.4174 vs a 12-sample floor is 3.33 million comparisons, not a near-miss).
   Phase 65 is the ranker/beam/all-settings path, gated on `--ostwald-curve`. No new unified stripe.

7. **Constellation arm — DONE, clean negative (Phase 56.1).** 24/24 dead, 0 raw stops over
   3.832×10¹¹ settings, 0 physical, 0 quarantine. Middle ring pinned leaves ~12–23% untested on
   the 40–67 letter spans; the swept version (~5–7 days) is **not** recommended next — Phase 56.2's
   unicity arithmetic ranks archival discovery above it. Original staging note follows.

   **Mechanism as landed (Phase 56).** Her doctrine that Neustadt and
   scuttling are linked stages, kept as independent strings, is now runnable: a constellation
   unions only the absolute board constraints of separate anchors and asserts no plaintext in the
   gap. This is what lets the 8-letter `NEUSTADT` contribute without ever being swept solo.
   `Fixtures/p1030680_wenselaers_procedure_neustadt_constellations.json` holds **24
   single-component rows, 27 edges / 7–9 loops**, priced at **3.83×10¹¹ settings ≈ 2.8 h**. It
   stays **behind item 8**: the preflight shows a 47–67 letter span leaves **~12–23%** of keys
   uncovered while the middle ring is pinned A, so these rows need the full-middle arm first.

No new RTL, Metal kernel, `MAX_EDGES`, or Future-bank width is justified. Before item 8 resumes,
complete item 0: report these findings and the planned full-middle scope to Selm and give her active
archive work a chance to alter the search space.
